# The Book of Curiosities of the Sciences and Marvels for the Eyes
<note>original: "Kitab Ghara'ib al-funun wa-mulah al-'uyun". This is a famous 11th-century anonymous treatise on cosmography, containing unique maps and astronomical diagrams.</note>

--- Page 4 ---
<meta>This page follows the title leaf of MS Arab. c. 90. Having established the ownership and title, the author now begins the formal introduction (the *khutbah*) and the table of contents for the work.</meta>

<margin>
My Lord, increase me in knowledge.
</margin>

->**In the name of God, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful**<-

Praise be to God, the One without an opponent to rival or negate Him, the Eternal without a peer to challenge or suffice for Him. He is the Originator of every thing and its Beginner; the Creator of every living soul and its Restorer. No regions can contain Him, nor can any eyes perceive Him. He is the Knower of all that occurs, the One who encompasses the secrets hidden within the breast. I offer Him praise that has no end to its beginning, and no conclusion to its end. May God’s blessings be upon the bearer of glad tidings of His repentance and mercy, and the warner of His punishment and vengeance—the pure choice and the guiding example, our Master Muhammad, the Chosen One (*al-Mustafa*). And may blessings be upon those he guided within his community: his family, the righteous chosen ones, and the excellent Caliphs; and may He grant them peace and honor.

You had asked me—may God grant you a share of every virtue and a lineage in every noble tradition, and may He make your fortune happy and your path righteous—to draft a book ————— encompassing the conditions of the **raised ceiling** <note>original: "al-saqf al-marfu‘". A Quranic metaphor (Surah 52:5) referring to the heavens or the celestial canopy.</note> and the **laid-out cradle** <note>original: "al-mihad al-mawdu‘". Another Quranic allusion (Surah 78:6) referring to the earth as a resting place for humanity.</note>. It shall reveal for you what has been obscured of their knowledge and clarify for you what was difficult regarding their states. Thus, I have fulfilled what you requested and composed that which you aimed for. I ask God Almighty to place this work where it meets your approval and matches your preference, for He is the Guardian of that and the One Capable of it. I have made ————— this book of mine, titled ***The Curiosities of the Sciences and Marvels for the Eyes***, into four treatises ———— whose chapters follow in succession and whose subjects are balanced. 

**The First Treatise** comprises the form of the highest heaven <note>original: "al-falak al-a‘la". In medieval cosmology, this is the outermost sphere that moves all others.</note>, its image, its conditions, and its dimensions; the twelve signs of the Zodiac; the seven planets; the mansions of the moon <note>original: "manazil al-qamar". The 28 divisions of the sky through which the moon passes.</note>, their appearances, and their examinations; the **long-haired stars** <note>original: "al-kawakib dhawat al-dhawa'ib". A poetic medieval term for comets, referring to their "tails" or "tresses."</note>, their effects, and their states; and everything that one needs to know or desires to understand concerning them. 

**The Second Treatise** comprises the **Seven Climes** <note>original: "al-aqalim al-sab‘". The classical system of dividing the inhabited world into seven latitudinal zones.</note>, their conditions, their latitudes, their seas, their islands, the measurements of their boundaries, their highlands, their lowlands, their famous rivers, and their celebrated lands. I have followed this with a mention of the wonders of the earth and its marvels, including distorted human creatures <note>original: "al-khalq al-insaniyya al-mushawwaha". Likely referring to legendary "monstrous races" common in medieval travelogues.</note> depicted there, as well as the silent beasts. This is accompanied by a mention of the causes of plant life, trees, and waters in every region. 

And there follows the first treatise:

<summary>This preface introduces the "Book of Curiosities," framing the work as a response to a patron's request for a comprehensive guide to the heavens and the earth. It outlines the first two treatises, covering celestial phenomena like comets and the geography of the "Seven Climes."</summary>
<keywords>cosmology, Muhammad, Zodiac, Seven Climes, comets, lunar mansions, monstrous races, medieval geography, Kitab Ghara'ib al-funun</keywords>

## The First Treatise, which consists of Ten Chapters
---

**The First Chapter** on the form of the celestial sphere, the nature of its shape, and its magnitude. **The Second Chapter** on the forms of the Northern and Southern zodiacal constellations and their conditions. **The Third Chapter** on the quantity of Northern stars and the Southern circles <note>Likely referring to the southern celestial constellations or rings of the armillary sphere.</note>. **The Fourth Chapter** on identifying the "hidden stars," conjunctions <term>al-ittisal: a technical term in astrology describing the application of one planet to another</term>, and their positions within natal charts and configurations. **The Fifth Chapter** on the illustrations of the aforementioned Northern and Southern stars. **The Sixth Chapter** on the conditions of comets <note>original: "dhawat al-dhawayib," literally "the ones with flowing locks or braids of hair," a common historical term for comets.</note> and the wonders associated with their rising. **The Seventh Chapter** on the hidden stars located within the Ninth Sphere <note>In the Ptolemaic system used by medieval scholars, the Ninth Sphere was the outermost heaven that moved all others.</note>, as well as their influences and effects. **The Eighth Chapter** on the states of the five planets <note>Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note> and the two luminaries—the Moon and the Sun—including their specific properties, traits, directions, actions, transformations, and their malefic <term>nahas: unfortunate or ill-omened</term> and benefic <term>sa'd: auspicious or lucky</term> qualities. **The Ninth Chapter** on the Lunar Mansions <term>manazil al-qamar: the 28 stations of the lunar cycle</term>, their forms, their descensions <note>original: "hubutat," referring to the "fall" or astrological debility of a celestial body.</note>, the dates and timings of their rising, and all necessary knowledge regarding their various states. **The Tenth Chapter** on the blowing of the winds from all directions and the atmospheric influences of which the scholar <note>original: "al- ‘alam," likely meaning the world or the learned observer.</note> must be aware.

->The chapters of the first treatise are complete; praise and gratitude be to God.<-

In the chapter on the seasons, we have provided this populated table derived from the "Table of the Great Celestial Sphere," including the seasons therein. Of these, there are three remaining: Summer, Autumn, and Winter.
The season of Spring is that which begins upon the completion of the Sun’s arrival at the head of the first sign of the twelve zodiac signs, which is the sign of Aries <note>original: "al-hamal," the Ram</note>.
The seasons in this circular diagram are divided into four sections, and each section consists of ninety days and a quarter of a day.
This is the preferred arrangement. Praise be to God alone, and through His help; and may God bless our master Muhammad, his family, and his companions, and grant them much peace.

This table is the one relied upon for extracting the "Inner Thoughts" <term>al-dama'ir: the hidden intentions of the seeker</term>, hidden things, and matters of the unseen. God knows best His own unseen realm and what is within it; He is the Mighty, the Wise.

->In the name of God, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful, and by Him is my success<-

## Chapter One: On the Nature of the Celestial Sphere

**And a summary of what the scholars have mentioned regarding its science and its structure, God willing.**

Indeed, the science of the celestial sphere, its conditions, its rotations, and its movements is a science that creatures are unable to fully grasp in its true essence and exact proportions. This is because the Most High, the Creator of all things, has reserved the knowledge of the unseen for Himself and is unique in His true will, separate from His creation—except for those He has chosen from among His prophets and selected from among His purified ones. He has bestowed upon whomever He willed from among them what He willed of His knowledge. It is said—and God knows best His own hidden things—that Idris <note>The Prophet Idris is traditionally identified with the biblical Enoch and the figure Hermes Trismegistus; he is credited in Islamic tradition with being the first to study the stars and the first to write.</note> (peace be upon him) was granted a hidden revelation regarding the science of celestial rulings, the paths of the luminous stars in the "uplifted roof" <note>original: al-Saqf al-Marfu’. A Quranic term (Surah At-Tur 52:5) referring to the sky or heaven as a protected ceiling.</note> placed upon the established cradle, along with their causes and their movements in their orbits and their offspring.

Thus, the Wise and All-Aware allowed the researcher to reflect and the seeker to be an eyewitness to the power of the One who "placed constellations in the sky and placed therein a lamp and a luminous moon," <note>A reference to Surah Al-Furqan 25:61. The "lamp" refers to the Sun.</note> and those who "reflect upon the creation of the heavens and the earth: 'Our Lord, You did not create this in vain; Glory be to You, so protect us from the punishment of the Fire.'" <note>Surah Ali 'Imran 3:191.</note>

I have explained the knowledge of this and summarized its science according to what has been spoken by the great philosophers and the eminent scholars who rose to prominence, such as:
*   **Ja'far Muhammad al-Balkhi** <note>Known in the West as Abu Ma'shar (787–886 AD), one of the most influential astrologers of the Middle Ages.</note>
*   **Muhammad ibn Ibrahim al-Fazari** <note>An 8th-century philosopher and astronomer who was the first in the Islamic world to construct an astrolabe.</note>
*   **Khalid ibn Abd al-Malik al-Marwazi**
*   **Muhammad ibn Musa al-Khwarizmi** <note>The famous mathematician and astronomer whose name gave us the word "algorithm."</note>
*   **Sanad ibn Ali**
*   **Habash Allah Abdullah** <note>Also known as Habash al-Hasib, a famous astronomer from Merv who worked in Baghdad.</note>
*   **Al-Hasan ibn Misbah**
*   **The Banu Nawbakht** <note>A famous Persian family of scholars and astrologers who served the Abbasid caliphs.</note>

...and others. I have brought forth a portion of what they mentioned and asked of what they explained; and with God is all success. As for what you asked regarding the perception of reality:

The scholars collectively maintain that the celestial sphere is a rounded body containing everything within it. It surrounds the Earth, which has length and width, and the Earth's position is in the center, like a point within a circle. The **Greatest Sphere** <term>Primum Mobile</term> <note>The outermost sphere in the geocentric model which moves all other spheres.</note> surrounds it, and its rotation is from East to West upon two different poles, South and North. Its science revolves through a natural movement by the will of its Creator, Designer, and Originator.

The greatest circles within it are the **Zodiacal Belt** <term>Ecliptic</term> and the circle of latitude. The starting point for counting them is from the great circle whose orbit is the center of the Earth and the movement of the sphere. It is called the **Circle of Inclination** <note>original: Da'irat al-Mayl.</note>, which intersects the region of the Greatest Sphere at two poles <unclear>by the third</unclear>. One of them is called the **Autumnal Equinox** point and the other is the **Vernal Equinox** point.

Then, inside the Greatest Sphere is the Sphere of the Zodiac. There is a disagreement regarding the center of the Sphere of the Zodiac: some say it is the center of the Earth itself, while others say it is outside of it, though close to it.

The knowledge of its <unclear>discovery</unclear>, its rising, its setting, and its <unclear>cycles</unclear> is that when a line is drawn from the center of the Earth to the side of the Circle of Inclination from the East, it constitutes a "year." From the Vernal Equinox point, at a measure of eight degrees toward the North, and when another line is drawn to its side toward the West reaching the Circle of Inclination, there is a <unclear>station</unclear> of eight degrees between it and the Vernal Equinox.

The Earth, compared to the celestial sphere, is like a point within it. The spheres surround it, and what holds the Earth is the body of the air, which is porous and concave on the inside and domed on the outside, and its <unclear>branches</unclear> contain the Earth.

Next follows the **Sphere of the Moon**, which is applied over the body of the air; its interior is also concave where it meets the air, and its nature is cold and moist.

Next follows the **Sphere of Mercury**, and its nature follows the previously mentioned example of concavity.

Next follows the **Sphere of Venus**, and its nature is cold and moist because it falls upon the <unclear>valleys</unclear>.

Next follows the **Sphere of the Sun**, which is the sphere of radiance, light, heat, and dryness, falling under moderation.

Next follows the **Sphere of Mars**, and its nature is fire, outside of moderation.

Next follows the **Sphere of Jupiter**...

<summary>This page introduces the geocentric cosmological model, framing astronomy as a divine gift revealed to the Prophet Idris. It lists famous medieval Islamic astronomers and begins describing the nested planetary spheres starting from the Earth outward to Jupiter.</summary>
<keywords>astronomy, cosmology, Idris, Enoch, Abu Ma'shar, al-Khwarizmi, Greatest Sphere, Zodiac, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Sun, Mars, Jupiter, geocentrism</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>The previous page introduced the celestial science as a divine gift bestowed upon the Prophet Idris (Enoch), framing astronomy as a spiritual and intellectual pursuit. This page continues the technical description of the spheres, specifically detailing the properties of the planets, the measurements of the Zodiac, and the classification of the signs according to seasons, elements, and qualities.</meta>

It is hot and moist, and its nature is that of life and the gentle breeze. Then it is followed by the sphere of Saturn, which is cold, dry, and bitter; it is seen as existing outside of moderation.

Then follows the sphere of the Zodiac <note>original: falak al-buruj</note>, which is composed of various natures and celestial bodies. It is divided into three hundred and sixty parts. The measurement of each part—according to what Al-Farghani <note>Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Kathir al-Farghani (d. 860 AD), known in the West as Alfraganus, was a celebrated astronomer in the Abbasid Caliphate who calculated the diameter of the Earth and the distances of the planets.</note> mentioned in the *Book of Chapters*—is one million, one hundred thousand, and one hundred and sixty miles. These degrees were then divided among twelve signs, with each sign containing thirty degrees. They are named: Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces.

The beginning of these is the sign of Aries, because it is the "First Head" <insert>due to its starting point at the celestial equator toward the north</insert>. It is followed sign by sign until the beginning of the second half, which starts with Libra, and from there begins the path toward the south. Furthermore, the celestial sphere is divided into two sections: hot and cold. The hot section spans from the beginning of Aries to the end of Virgo, while the cold section spans from the beginning of Libra to the end of Pisces. The influences of the superior planets <note>The "superior planets" in traditional astronomy are those further from the Sun than Earth: Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note> are stronger when they are in the northern signs than when they are in the southern signs. The sphere was also divided into two unequal parts: the "Greater Division" from Cancer to Capricorn, and the "Lesser Division" from Capricorn to Cancer.

The sphere was then divided into four quarters:

*   **The First Quarter:** From Aries to Cancer. It is called the infantile or youthful quarter; it is naturally hot and moist.
*   **The Second Quarter:** From Cancer to Libra. It is called the adolescent or growing quarter; it is hot and dry.
*   **The Third Quarter:** From Libra to Capricorn. It is called the mature quarter, the beginning of decline; it is cold, dry, autumnal, and melancholic <note>original: sawdawi. Refers to "black bile," one of the four humors in medieval medicine associated with the autumn season and a dry, cold temperament.</note>.
*   **The Fourth Quarter:** From Capricorn to Aries. It is called the decaying, senile, or vanishing quarter; it is the wintry quarter.

These signs were also categorized into two types: those that are "Straight" in their rising and those that are "Crooked" in their rising. The crooked signs "twist" matters and corrupt them; these range from the beginning of Capricorn to the end of Gemini, and they are subservient to the straight signs. The straight signs make matters easy and distinct, and they command the crooked ones; these range from the beginning of Cancer to the end of Capricorn.

Furthermore, these signs were categorized according to the four natures:

1.  **Fiery:** Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius.
2.  **Earthly:** Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn.
3.  **Airy:** Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius.
4.  **Watery:** Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces.

Then these signs were divided into three classes based on their actions:
*   **Fixed:** Those that stabilize a season, indicating a single nature. These are Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius.
*   **Mutable:** <note>original: dhu jasadayn (literally "two-bodied").</note> Those in which the temperament of the season is linked to another. These are Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces.
*   **Cardinal:** <note>original: munqalib (literally "turning").</note> Those where the season turns from one type to another. These are Aries, Cancer, and Libra <note>Capricorn is typically included in this group as the fourth cardinal sign.</note>.

Then the "Greatest Peak" was established because within these signs are the <unclear>governors</unclear> of the planets within their bounds. The Sun was assigned six signs, from the beginning of Leo to the end of Capricorn, which constitutes the larger half of the zodiac belt. The Moon <unclear>and its folk</unclear> were granted a place across from the Sun on the other half, from the beginning of Aquarius to the end of Cancer, which is the smaller half. 

The sphere rotates in <unclear>twenty-four hours</unclear>, completing one full cycle. All other spheres rotate along with it because they are held within it through eternity. There are eight spheres for the heavenly bodies: the first of them is called the Highest, which is the sphere of the "Fixed Stars" seen in the sky. The other seven spheres belong to the wandering planets. Including everything documented by science, there are one thousand and twenty-two <note>This is the classic number of stars cataloged in Ptolemy's Almagest, which remained the standard for centuries.</note> fixed stars that do not move independently; they are like nails fixed into a <term>water-wheel</term> <note>original: dulab. A mechanical wheel, often a water-wheel or gear-wheel; used here to describe how stars are fixed into the rotating fabric of the sphere.</note> according to their proportions.

<summary>This page outlines the mathematical and elemental structure of the Zodiac, detailing the 360-degree division, the four seasonal quadrants, and the classification of signs by element and quality. It cites the astronomer Al-Farghani and describes the celestial spheres as a mechanical system where stars are fixed like nails on a great wheel.</summary>

<keywords>Zodiac, Al-Farghani, celestial spheres, four elements, cardinal signs, fixed stars, planetary rulership, medieval astronomy, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the cosmological survey from the previous page, moving from the spheres of the planets to the eighth sphere—the firmament of the fixed stars. It bridges mathematical astronomy with historical and legendary accounts of how this knowledge was preserved by the Greeks, Chaldeans, and Indians.</meta>

Those fixed stars scattered throughout the eighth sphere are nineteen million and ninety parasangs <note>term: parasang - an ancient unit of distance, roughly equal to 3 to 4 miles or the distance a person can walk in an hour.</note> from the Earth. Among the bodies of those stars, there are fifteen that are equal to the Sun in magnitude. The circumference of each of these stars is seventeen thousand, three hundred and fifty-five parasangs.

The diameter of the Highest Sphere—according to what Al-Farghani <note>Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Kathir al-Farghani (d. 860 AD), known in the West as Alfraganus, was a primary authority on astronomy whose works influenced both the Islamic world and the European Renaissance.</note> mentioned in the *Book of Chapters* in the twenty-first chapter—is one hundred and one million, fifteen thousand miles. If that figure is multiplied by three and one-seventh <note>The Archimedean approximation of Pi (π).</note>, the result is the circumference of the Greatest Sphere, which is four hundred and ten million, eighteen thousand, five hundred and seven miles. As for the area of each degree of its sphere, it is two hundred thousand and sixty-eight miles.

Thon of Alexandria <note>Theon of Alexandria (c. 335–405 AD), the last director of the Library of Alexandria and father of Hypatia.</note> and a group of Chaldeans <note>The Chaldeans were famous astronomers and astrologers of ancient Mesopotamia.</note> claimed that the celestial sphere possesses degrees of "advancing" and "receding" <note>This refers to the theory of "trepidation," an obsolete astronomical model that suggested the equinoxes moved back and forth rather than in a continuous precession.</note>. They held that whenever works are performed during the degrees of "advancing," they endure and are made eternal; but when they are performed during the degrees of "receding," they are erased and pass away. The general movement of the Zodiac sphere within the Greatest Sphere <margin>on</margin> its advancing path toward the East and its receding path toward the West is eight degrees. The rate of this movement is one degree every sixty Egyptian years. Its limit is every five hundred and sixty years, and each of these years consists of three hundred and sixty-five days. The masters of Talismans mention that this movement, according to them, is one degree every sixty-nine years, reaching its limit of eight degrees in four hundred and forty-eight years. In true speech, however, this claim is far from correct, as the Zodiac sphere functions and rotates upon its own face. Since this is the case, the movements calculated over two different long spans must be proportional to one another. It is our duty to report what these people have mentioned; if they were correct, it is by their merit, and if they erred, it is by their ignorance.

As for the wisdom of India, they all mention that the derivation of the science of the stars from the circulating books was achieved by the King of Fanuh <note>Likely a reference to the city of Kannauj or the legendary astronomical center of Ujjain, often called "Arin" in medieval Arabic geography.</note>. His city is located on the Equator; it is one of the largest cities of India and the most noble in its layout. Its latitude from the celestial equator toward the North is three degrees. It is the city of the sages and the refuge of Indian scholars. The observation of the stars in this city took place four hundred years before the Divine Mission <note>Referring to the start of the Prophet Muhammad's mission (c. 610 AD).</note>, and they applied themselves with great diligence to its science.

Others said that when the King of India saw the stars, he ordered the scholars to seize the opportunity to observe them and to determine their mean motions and their <term>kardajas</term> <note>kardaja - a Sanskrit-derived term (from *kramajya*) used in medieval Arabic astronomy to refer to segments of the sine or arc-segments used in trigonometric tables.</note>. Whatever he found that corresponded to the sciences he inherited from his ancestors in their observations, they developed further. The King, whose name was Dahumuha, adopted them and set them according to the years of the era of Abraham. He ordered that whatever was calculated from them at any time should be used as a date to verify the day. It was built of gold upon tablets of ivory. He lived to be one hundred and thirteen years old, having observed the stars for ninety years. He was self-sufficient in this work due to his great skill, until a wise scholar from the farthest reaches of his land, known as Shular <note>Possible corruption of "Siddhanta," the Sanskrit astronomical treatises brought to the Abbasid court.</note>, came to him. He brought him the mean motions and the *kardajas*, mentioning that he found them buried in the treasuries of the infant kings. His reign was six hundred years before the Divine Mission. The King accepted them when he saw their accuracy and their agreement with what he had organized and labored upon; thus, he issued them from his House of Wisdom <gloss>upon it</gloss>.

He built it in his kingdom, and the first of the seven stations was the city of Fanuh, with seventy *kardajas* attributed to the history of India, and he is the Greatest Sage. They claim that when the stars are calculated by these means and their mean motions, they lead to the truths which they wrote down, proved, and preserved in the Great House of Idols <note>original: bayt al-asnam</note>. None may look upon them except the Brahmin <note>original: al-Brahimi</note> among their worshippers. The Brahmin in charge of the House of Idols brings out a book for him every day containing the course of the seven planets and the states of their movements—including their conjunctions and oppositions—and the relations of the wandering stars. They receive this from him and learn what they have mastered through the rigorous ranks of wisdom <unclear>as they wish</unclear>.

...benefit from it on that day until midday, then it was hidden in its storehouses so that it would be known that it does not appear in the count of that day, as was explained at the end of the description of its state.

We return now to the mention of the **Zodiac Signs** <note>Arabic: *al-Buruj*, literally "towers," referring to the twelve constellations of the zodiac.</note> and their characteristics. Among them are the **masculine** and the **feminine**. As for the **masculine** ones, which are **diurnal** <note>term: diurnal - associated with the day or active during daylight hours.</note>, they are **Aries**, **Gemini**, **Leo**, **Libra**, **Sagittarius**, and **Aquarius**. As for the **feminine** ones, which are **nocturnal** <note>term: nocturnal - associated with the night or active during the night hours.</note>, they are **Taurus**, **Cancer**, **Virgo**, **Scorpio**, **Capricorn**, and **Pisces**.

As for the **quartiles** <note>Groups of three signs corresponding to the four seasons.</note>, they are **Aries**, **Taurus**, and **Gemini** [Spring]; then the classified ones: **Cancer**, **Leo**, and **Virgo** [Summer]; the **Autumnal** ones: **Libra**, **Scorpio**, and **Sagittarius**; and the **Winter** ones: **Capricorn**, **Aquarius**, and **Pisces**.

In these, there are signs that are hostile to other signs. Those hostile to the rest of the signs are **Aries**, **Leo**, and **Sagittarius** <note>These are the Fire signs.</note>; likewise, those hostile to the rest are **Gemini**, **Libra**, and **Aquarius** <note>These are the Air signs.</note>; likewise, those hostile to the rest are **Cancer**, **Scorpio**, and **Pisces** <note>These are the Water signs.</note>; and likewise, those hostile to the rest are **Taurus**, <margin>This is called the *Kadkhuda* <note>A Persian term used in medieval astrology for the "Giver of Life" or "Lord of the House," a planet that was thought to determine the length of a person's life.</note></margin> **Virgo**, and **Capricorn** <note>These are the Earth signs.</note>. Their mutual hostility arises from the differences in their elemental natures.

As for the assignment of a specific planet to each sign, we present it according to the truth of the matter as decreed by the ancient scholars. They said that God the Almighty assigned the **Greatest Luminary** <note>The Sun.</note> specifically to the sign of **Leo**, and assigned the **Moon** to the sign of **Cancer**. Each of these two signs became a residence, and each residence was specifically associated with its owner among the planets. He—Exalted and Majestic—created the planets from the Sun and Moon, made red by the light and radiance of the Sun, and they retreated, moving away from it due to the intensity of its light. Their lights extended from the houses of the **Two Luminaries** <note>The Sun and the Moon.</note> to where each planet settled at the limit of its retreat.

Each planet, in its retreat, moved one hundred and fifty degrees from its place. Starting from the sign of **Leo**, it ended at the seventh sign, which is **Aquarius**, where it settled and it became its house. If you count these degrees from the sign of **Cancer**, the seventh limit is also **Capricorn**, by the same measure of degrees. Thus, these two became the houses of **Saturn** <note>Arabic: *Zuhal*.</note> and his domain.

Then the chord of **Jupiter** <note>Arabic: *al-Mushtari*.</note> was one hundred and fifty degrees. If you count five signs from **Leo** to their degrees (one hundred and fifty), you find the sign of **Sagittarius**, so it became the house of Jupiter. If you count backwards from the sign of **Cancer** five signs—saying Cancer, Gemini, Taurus, Aries, and Pisces—we reach the fifth sign, and Jupiter was placed there.

The chord of **Mars** <note>Arabic: *al-Marrikh*.</note> was one hundred and twenty degrees. If you count straight from **Leo**, you find the fourth sign is **Scorpio**, so it became the house of Mars. If you count backwards from **Cancer**, you find the sign of **Aries**, so it also became a house of Mars and his domain.

The chord of **Venus** <note>Arabic: *al-Zuhrah*.</note> was ninety degrees, which is three signs. If you count from **Leo** at an equal distance, the count reaches from its house to the sign of **Libra**, so it became the house of Venus. If you count backwards from the sign of **Cancer**, the count reaches the sign of **Taurus**, so it also became the house of Venus.

The chord of **Mercury** <note>Arabic: *Utarid*.</note> was sixty degrees, which is one sign <note>The text says "one sign" but Mercury is 60 degrees/2 signs away.</note>. If you count from the sign of **Leo**, it is the second, which is **Virgo**, so it became a house for Mercury. If you count backwards from the sign of **Cancer**, you find **Gemini**, so it also became Mercury’s specific house. For this reason, the planets were lightened by these signs based on the extent of their light's emergence and their proximity to the light of the Sun.

Our predecessors among the philosophers also provided a graceful analogy for these planets. They said the **Two Luminaries** <note>The Sun and Moon.</note> are, in status, like two **Kings**. Every king must have a **Vizier** <note>A high official or minister.</note> to whom he refers. The Sun took **Mercury** as a vizier from its second house, and the Moon took **Mercury** as a vizier from its second house in reverse. Thus, Mercury obtained two houses on both sides of the Luminaries. Then, the King must have a companion to find comfort with, so the Sun assigned **Venus** to what follows it, and the Moon assigned **Venus** to what follows it in reverse. Then they said the King must have a **Master of the Sword** <note>A general or military commander.</note> to maintain awe, so the Sun assigned...

...from the fourth sign from the Sun, which is assigned to **Mars** <note>Arabic: *al-Marikh*</note>, and specifically the Moon’s fourth sign in reverse <note>This refers to the symmetrical assignment of planetary "houses" starting from the Sun (Leo) and Moon (Cancer).</note>, making it a house for **Mars**. Furthermore, a King <note>Referring to the Sun as the "King" of the heavens.</note> must have a **judge** <note>term: judge - here an astrological metaphor for Jupiter (*al-Mushtari*), the planet of justice and wisdom.</note> to rule over his subjects; thus, I assigned to **Jupiter** a house from them, the sign of **Sagittarius**. So it became **Jupiter’s**, and specifically the Moon’s eighth in reverse—the fifth sign of **Pisces**—which also became a house for **Jupiter**. Then, the King must have a **bearer of armor**, weapons, and fortifications; thus, I assigned the sixth sign from the Sun, the sign of **Capricorn**, to **Saturn** <note>Arabic: *Zuhal*</note>, and specifically the Moon’s sixth sign in reverse, **Aquarius**, which also became **Saturn’s**. And God knows best.

## Chapter Two: On Knowing the Characteristics of the Zodiac Signs

The first of them is named **Aries** <note>Arabic: *al-Hamal*</note>, and its name in Greek is *Krios* <note>The transcription says "Karbos," likely a scribal corruption of the Greek *Krios*.</note>. Its image is that of a ram with its neck turned back, stepping toward the East as if looking behind it, resting its head on its back. Its head faces the North Pole. Its body is balanced right and left in a manner resembling **Saturn** <note>Arabic: *Zuhal*</note>. It has the leg of a horse and the tail of a horse. It is the house <term>house - the zodiac sign over which a planet has primary influence</term> of **Mars** <note>Arabic: *al-Marikh*</note> and the exaltation <term>exaltation - the specific degree where a planet’s power is at its peak</term> of the **Sun** at nineteen degrees. It is the detriment <term>detriment - the sign opposite a planet's home, where its influence is weakened</term> of **Venus** <note>Arabic: *al-Zuharah*</note>. 

According to the Egyptians, it has five bounds <term>bounds - five unequal segments of a sign ruled by different planets</term>: **Jupiter**, **Venus**, **Mercury**, **Mars**, and **Saturn**. It has three faces <term>faces - ten-degree divisions of a sign, also known as decans</term>: the first belongs to **Mars**, then the **Sun**, then **Venus**. 

It belongs to the triplicity <term>triplicity - a group of three signs belonging to the same element, in this case Fire</term> of the **Sun**, **Jupiter**, and **Saturn**. It has three subdivisions of **Saturn**, **Mars**, and the **Sun**, and three further degrees of the **Moon**, **Mercury**, and **Venus**. It has seven periods <note>Arabic: *nawbat*</note>. Among the human limbs, it governs the face, the head, and whatever ailments occur within them. 

Among cities and lands, it governs Babylon, Persia, Azerbaijan, Palestine, the island of Cyprus, the coast of Lower Khorasan, the land of the Slavs, Khilat, and Mosul. Among the specific degrees, the nineteenth degree possesses ultimate happiness <note>referring back to the exaltation of the Sun</note>. The fourth and fifth degrees are "bright." The first and eighth degrees are "dark." The second and seventh degrees are "feminine." The degrees that are "luminous to the sight" are the sixth, eleventh, sixteenth, twenty-third, twenty-sixth, and twenty-ninth. 

Its nature is fiery, eastern, diurnal, masculine, cardinal <note>Arabic: *munqalib* - literally 'turning', marking the start of a season</note>, hot, and dry, associated with yellow bile. Among the lunar mansions <term>lunar mansions - the 28 stations of the Moon's monthly cycle</term>, it contains *al-Sharatayn*, *al-Butayn*, and one-third of *al-Thurayya* (the Pleiades). It marks the increase of daylight over the hours of the equinox and is "rising in its ascensions." 

It governs the color yellow and the pungent <note>literally: burning/acrid</note> taste. It hates **Virgo**, **Scorpio**, and **Pisces**, and loves **Leo** and **Sagittarius**. Among the parts of a ship, it governs the bow. Among animals, it governs sheep. Among people, it governs servants and attendants. Among days, it governs Tuesday. Among emotional states, it governs joy. Among the months, it governs *Nisan* (April), and of the seasons, it governs Spring. It is "crooked" in its rising, having a "half-voice," and its rising takes twenty degrees. It is hot in the Eastern horizon—the horizon of the **Sun** and **Mars**—but contrary to them in the West. 

Regarding its stars, there are thirteen: two in its horn, one on its neck, one on its back, one on its side, and its eye is on its rump <note>This description likely refers to specific Ptolemaic star positions</note>. It is a hidden star, one above its middle, and one on its back. Among the remaining stars, there is a star that rises in three degrees of it and seven... its northern latitude is thirteen. It holds the first honor, and among its friends are **Venus** and **Jupiter**.

**Taurus** <note>Arabic: *al-Thawr*</note>, its name in Greek is *Tauros* and in Persian is *Gaw*. Its image is that of a bull with its rear lowered, in a posture within the area of **Gemini** toward the South, descending while attempting to rise, with its two legs hidden toward the South. One of its ears is toward the North and the other toward the South. It is the house of **Venus**, the exaltation of the **Moon** at three degrees, and the detriment of **Mars**. 

It has five bounds according to the Egyptians: **Mercury**, **Jupiter**, **Saturn**, and **Mars**. Its faces are **Mercury**, the **Moon**, and **Saturn**. It belongs to the triplicity of **Venus**, the **Moon**, and **Mars**. Its subdivisions belong to **Saturn**, the **Moon**, and **Mercury**, and its degrees to the **Moon**, **Mercury**, and **Venus**. It has nine periods. 

Among the lunar mansions, it contains the "belly" of *al-Thurayya* (the Pleiades), *al-Dabaran*, and is followed by *al-Haq'ah*. Among human limbs, it governs the neck and the throat. Among regions, it governs the *Sawad* (Lower Mesopotamia), the Mahat, Hamadan, the Kurds, and Isfahan. Among the lands of the snakes, it governs the land of... <unclear>Maghdbah</unclear>, the island of Cyprus, and the coast of Lower Khorasan...

Among the bright stars, there are three: the 3rd, 15th, and 27th. Of the bright degrees, there are the 3rd, 7th, and 8th. The dark degrees are eight in number. There are two masculine degrees: the 7th and 21st. There is one feminine degree: the 8th. Of the degrees harmful to eyesight <note>Traditional astrology often associated specific degrees with ocular health or visual impairment</note>, there are six: the 11th, 16th, 21st, 23rd, and 29th. Of the fourth degree of fortune, there are three: the 3rd, 15th, and 27th.

Among the fixed stars is the "Eye of the Bull" <note>original: *Ain al-Thawr*</note>, which is **Aldebaran**, located at twenty-three degrees of it. Appearing with it are the "Wrist of the Giant" <note>original: *Mu‘sam al-Jabbar*, referring to a star in the constellation Orion</note> and the "Demon’s Head" <note>original: *Ras al-Ghul*, known in the West as Algol</note>. 

It <note>Taurus</note> is feminine, earthy, southern, spring-like, and dry. Its constitution is dry and associated with black bile <term>black bile - one of the four humors in ancient medicine, associated with a melancholic temperament</term>. Its taste is sour. It governs the number eight. Among colors, it is the color of the sky. Among the temperaments, it is associated with phlegm. It shares traits with parts of the signs of Libra, Sagittarius, Aries, Virgo, and Ophiuchus <note>original: *al-Hawwa*</note>. It is swift in its lower part near the chest. Among the days, it belongs to Friday. Among breads, it is the dappled or multicolored kind. Among stones, it is onyx <note>original: *al-jaza‘*</note>. Among clothing, it is black and hairy garments. Among emotions, it is sorrow. Among the humors, it is black bile. It is of "crooked ascent" <term>crooked ascent - a sign that takes less time to rise over the horizon than others</term>, rising in the first of two hours. Among the eight stars is "The Rose" <note>original: *al-Warda*</note> at eight and ten degrees of it, which is northern. Aldebaran is at twenty-three degrees and ten minutes, being southern. There is a star at twenty-three degrees and another at twenty-seven degrees. It is "maimed of limbs," sterile, and hot in the East, agreeing with **Jupiter** and **Mars**. In the West, it agrees with **Venus**. It has no voice.

### **The Sign of Gemini**

Its name in Persian is *Dipaykar* <note>The transcription says "Dinkar," a corruption of the Persian *Du-paykar*, meaning "Two-Figures"</note> and in Greek it is *Didymoi* <note>The transcription says "Zanmru," likely a scribal error for the Greek term for the Twins</note>. It is the triplicity of **Mercury** <note>original: *Utarid*</note>. The exaltation of the "Head" <term>Head - the North Node of the Moon, or Rahu</term> is at three degrees of it, and the fall of the "Tail" <term>Tail - the South Node of the Moon, or Ketu</term> is at three degrees of it. It is the house of **Mercury**. 

It is of crooked ascent and hot on the eastern horizon, dry on the western horizon, cold, moist, and sanguine <term>sanguine - the humor of blood, associated with a social and active temperament</term>. It is masculine and "dual-bodied" <term>dual-bodied - also known as mutable signs, representing transition</term>. It increases the length of the day. It is vocal. It has five bounds: **Mercury**, **Jupiter**, **Venus**, **Mars**, and **Saturn**. It has three faces <note>decans</note>: **Jupiter**, **Mars**, and the **Sun**. It has the submissions <note>planetary transferences</note> of **Saturn**, **Mercury**, and **Jupiter**, and three degrees for **Mercury**, **Venus**, and **Saturn**. It has houses for the **Sun**, **Mars**, and **Jupiter**, and nine minor houses. 

Among the Lunar Mansions <term>Lunar Mansions - the 28 divisions of the sky the moon passes through each month</term>, it contains three: *Al-Haq‘ah*, *Al-Han‘ah*, and *Al-Dhira‘*. Regarding the body, it governs the shoulders, the upper arms, and the forearms. Among countries, it governs Armenia, Gorgan, Azerbaijan, Egypt, Daylam, Hayyan, Tabaristan, and the regions of Isfahan and Kerman. 

Among the fixed stars are the "Eye of the Goat" <note>Capella</note>, the "Shoulder of the Giant" <note>Betelgeuse</note>, the stars in the "Ship" <note>the constellation Argo Navis</note>, the "Foot of the Giant" <note>Rigel</note>, the "Navel of Gemini," the "Groom" <note>*Al-‘Ayyuq*, also Capella</note>, and *Suhayl* <note>Canopus</note>. 

Of the degrees of increasing fortune, there is one: the 11th. Of the bright degrees, there are three: the 4th, 5th, and 8th. The dark degree is the 8th. There is one "putrid" degree and one "mixed" degree. Of the masculine degrees, there are two: the 1st and 4th. Of the feminine, there are two: the 3rd and 5th. Of those harmful to eyesight, there are five: the 8th, 11th, 16th, 26th, and 30th. 

It is a masculine, diurnal, Arabic, sanguine, spring-like, hot, moist, and airy sign. Its taste is sweet. It is said to drive the rivers, and its person loves the color green. Its nature tends toward yellow bile <note>original: *al-marra al-safra*</note>. It shares traits with Sagittarius, Pisces, Taurus, Libra, and Aquarius. In the constellation of the Ship, it governs the "Wing." Among the days, it belongs to Wednesday. Among animals, it governs gray or dappled horses. Among jewels, it is the ruby. Among clothing, it is white. Among actions, it is laughter. 

Among the eight stars, the "Stained Hand" <note>original: *Kaff al-Khadib*, a star in Cassiopeia</note> rises in its first degree with a southern latitude. The "Stained Hand" also appears at nineteen degrees with a southern latitude. A star <unclear>Lugh</unclear> is at thirty-six degrees with a northern latitude. A star called "The Dog" <note>likely Sirius or Procyon</note> rises at twenty-one degrees with a northern latitude, and another star called "The Dog" appears as well.

It rises at twenty-one degrees and its breadth remains with it, its latitude being southern. A star named **Rigel** <note>original: *Rawn*, likely a transcription of *Rijil*, referring to Rigel in the constellation Orion which aligns with the later degrees of Gemini</note> rises at twenty-seven degrees of it, and its latitude is northern. This diurnal sign has a period of ascension of twenty-eight degrees.

### The Sign of Cancer

Its name in Persian is *Kharsheng* or *Sharok*, and in Roman <note>Greek</note> it is *Karkinos* <note>original: *Qarqalus*</note>. It is the House of the Moon, the place of the exaltation of Jupiter at fifteen degrees, the fall of Mars at fifteen degrees, and the detriment of Saturn. It is feminine, "moveable" <term>moveable sign - also known as a cardinal sign, it marks the beginning of a season (the Summer Solstice)</term>, nocturnal, and southern. It is called "moveable" because of the transition of time within it from Spring to Summer. It is associated with the humor of phlegm. It is increasing in daylight and increasing in its ascensions, which last thirty-two and one-third degrees. It is hot in the East, agreeing with Jupiter and Mars, while in the West it is "sharp" and their twin. It has a straight ascension. 

Among its influences are weeping and the building of many various forms. It is rough to the touch and not easily bent. Its image is the form of a crab with eight legs; one of its claws is on the northern side and the other is on the southern side. 

It has five "terms" <term>terms - specific degrees within a sign ruled by different planets</term> belonging to Mars, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn. It has three "faces" <term>faces - also known as decans, ten-degree subdivisions of a sign</term> belonging to Venus, Mercury, and the Moon. Among the triplicities, its three rulers are Venus, Mars, and the Moon. Among the degrees, the "soft" ones belong to the Moon, Mars, and Jupiter. Among the [other] degrees, the three belong to Saturn, the Sun, and Mercury. It has nine *Boharat* <note>original: *Boharat*, a term derived from the Sanskrit *Navamsa*, representing a nine-fold division of the sign</note>, the first of which is the Moon. 

Among the lunar mansions <term>lunar mansions - 28 divisions of the sky the moon passes through each month</term>, it contains *al-Nathrah* (the Crib), *al-Tarf* (the Gaze), and one-third of *al-Jabhah* (the Brow). Regarding the body, it governs the chest, the spine, the stomach, and the ribs. Among the fixed stars, it contains **Sirius** <note>original: *al-Shi‘ra al-Yamaniyah*</note>. 

Among the "extra" degrees of fortune are the first, 100th <note>unclear: possibly a transcription error for a lower number as signs only have 30 degrees</note>, 3rd, 14th, and 15th. Of the bright degrees, there are two: the 5th and 6th. Of the dark degrees, there are two: the 100th <note>unclear</note> and the 4th. Of the "valued" degrees, there is one: the 7th. Of the masculine degrees: the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, and 11th. Of the feminine degrees: the 4th and 5th. Of the degrees harmful to eyesight, known as "the Wells" <note>specific degrees believed to indicate potential blindness or eye disease</note>, there are seven: the 10th, 13th, 17th, 20th, 23rd, 26th, and 30th. 

Its color is dusty and it is considered masculine in color. Its qualities include cold, phlegm, and southern winds. It blends with the signs of Sagittarius, Aquarius, and Pisces. It is associated with the tail of Scorpio, Pisces, and the "stern of the Ship" <note>referring to the constellation Argo Navis</note> and the ribs. Among the days, it belongs to Monday. Among animals, it rules beasts. Among jewels, it is the emerald. Among clothing, it is green. Among actions, it governs marriage. It is dark at its end. Its season is Summer, its month is July <note>original: *Tammuz*</note>, and its direction is <margin>The Levant</margin>.

### The Sign of Leo

Its name in Persian is *Shadwa* or *Sakhushah* <gloss>his face</gloss>, and in Roman it is *Leon*. Its image is the form of a lion with its mane and back toward the North Pole and its face toward the equator, spreading its paws, arms, and legs as if reclining. It is a masculine, eastern, diurnal, and "fixed" sign <term>fixed sign - representing the middle and most stable part of a season</term>. It is the House of the Sun. It has no exaltation or fall, but it is the detriment of Saturn. 

It has five terms belonging to Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, and Mars. It has three faces: Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars. It has three triplicities: the Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn. It has twelve degrees belonging to Jupiter, Mars, and Venus, and three *adrajanat* <note>another term for decans or subdivisions</note> belonging to Venus, the Sun, and Mars. It has nine *Boharat* (Navamsas), the first of which is the Sun. 

Among the lunar mansions, it contains what follows *al-Jabhah* (the Brow), *al-Zubrah* (the Mane), and two-thirds of *al-Sarfah* (the Changer). In the human body, it governs the heart, the sides, the loins, and the back. Among lands, it governs the land of the Turks, up to the borders of Iraq, Sogdia, and Nishapur. It also governs Antioch, <unclear>Safalah</unclear>, and Chaldean *al-Mazzah*. 

Its season is Summer, and its month is August <note>original: *Ab*</note>. Its direction is East, and its day is Sunday. Among the fixed stars, it contains **Regulus** <note>original: *Qalb al-Asad* (the Heart of the Lion)</note>, the Neck of the Hydra <note>original: *Unq al-Shuja‘*</note>, and the "Manger." From it are the degrees...

Fourth, regarding happiness, there are four [degrees]: the 3rd, 5th, 6th, and 9th. Among the bright degrees are the 7th and 9th. Among the dark degrees is the 6th. Among the feminine degrees are the 3rd and 10th. Among the masculine are three: the 4th, 6th, and 7th. Among the feminine are two: the 3rd and 10th <note>These degree assignments are specific to medieval astrology, often used to determine the "strength" of a planet located there.</note>. Among the dark degrees that are harmful to the eyesight—known as "The Wells"—are five, four of which concern health. 

**Leo** <note>original: *al-Asad*</note> includes the 6th, 13th, 15th, and 22nd degrees, as well as **Regulus** <note>original: *Qalb al-Asad*, literally "The Heart of the Lion"</note>. It possesses half a voice, and its period of ascension is thirty-six degrees. Its corresponding clothing is yellow, its colors are dusky, and its temperament is phlegmatic <term>phlegm - one of the four bodily humors thought to influence health and personality</term>. Regarding the parts of the ship <note>Astrological texts often mapped the zodiac onto a ship or a human body for metaphorical or divinatory purposes</note>, it represents the upper deck. In terms of burdens, it is the lip/edge. Its gemstone is the **Ruby** <note>original: *Yaqut*</note>. Its characteristic action is contentment. It is increasing in daylight. Its associated lands are **Toledo**, **Apulia**, **Homs**, **Armenia**, and the fertile lands of **Kufa**.

## The Sign of Virgo
Its name in Persian is *Bazaruh* and in Roman <note>Greek</note> it is *Rabas* <note>original: *رباس*, likely a corruption of *Parthenos*</note>. Its image is that of a young girl with two wings; her head is joined to the "Blood of the Lion" <note>This refers to Virgo following the constellation Leo in the sky</note>, and the tips of her wings enter the Sign of Libra. Along the edge of her arms, her hands are outstretched, holding two ears of corn <note>Spica, the brightest star in Virgo, is represented as an ear of grain</note>. It is a "Double-bodied" <term>double-bodied - a sign that marks the end of a season, also called "mutable"; it represents transition</term> sign, airy and earthy, a summer sign increasing in daylight. It is associated with the humor of yellow bile and the North. It is the House of **Mercury**, its exaltation <term>exaltation - the sign where a planet's influence is most harmoniously strengthened</term> is at fifteen degrees, the fall of **Venus** is at seventeen degrees, and the detriment of **Jupiter**. 

It has five "terms" <term>terms - specific sections of a sign ruled by different planets</term> belonging to Mercury, Venus, Jupiter, Mars, and Saturn. It has three "faces" <term>faces - also known as decans, ten-degree subdivisions of a sign</term>: the Sun, Venus, and Mercury. Its three triplicities are ruled by Venus, Mars, and Jupiter [by day], and Mercury, Venus, and Saturn [by night]. It has nine "gates" <note>original: *bawabāt*, likely referring to the Navamsas/ninth-parts of the sign</note>, the first of which is Mercury. 

In terms of the lunar mansions <term>lunar mansions - 28 divisions of the sky the moon passes through each month</term>, it contains three: *al-Sarfah*, *al-‘Awwa*, and *al-Simak* <note>Spica</note>. From the body, it rules the intestines, the belly, the bowels, and the diaphragm. Among the "Hundred Stars," it contains the "Tail of the Lion" <note>Denebola</note>. 

Of the degrees that increase happiness, there are three: the 3rd, 13th, and 20th. Among the luminous degrees are the 6th and 7th. The 4th is a "technical" degree, and the 3rd is a dark degree. The 8th is an "empty" degree. The masculine degrees are the 5th and 10th, while the feminine degrees are the 6th and 8th. There are five degrees harmful to the eyesight: the 8th, 13th, 16th, 21st, and 25th. 

It does not have "vocal" colors. Its nature influences gas in the core, the stomach, and the intestines. It shares traits with the signs of **Aquarius**, **Aries**, and **Leo**, and is the ruler of **Capricorn** and **Taurus**. Regarding the ship, it represents the bottom (the keel). Its day is Wednesday. Its mountain is the Mottled Mountain. Its gemstone is **Onyx**. Its associated humor is anger. It is physically well-formed, "barren" <note>referring to its influence on fertility in agricultural or medical astrology</note>, and has a straight ascension of forty degrees. It is hot in the East, agreeing with those in the East. In the Western horizon, it agrees with Venus. The Southern stars rise within it: one at seventeen degrees North, and another at twenty-eight degrees North. To its left (South) is a northern star at seven degrees and twenty minutes; on its right is a star at fifteen degrees and thirty minutes North. Its lands are **Babylon**, the **Meeting of the Two Rivers** <note>Mesopotamia</note>, **Ajanah**, **Cordoba**, **Ahvaz**, the land of **Mosul**, and **Upper Mesopotamia** <note>al-Jazira</note>.

## The Sign of Libra
Its name in Persian is *Kardam* and in Roman <note>Greek</note> it is *Zygos*. Its image is that of a scale with two pans, a beam, and a pointer <note>original: *lisan*, literally "tongue"</note>, set within the beak of a "half-bird" <note>Likely referring to the proximity of the constellation Corvus</note>... [the text describes the alignment toward the North]. 

It is the House of **Venus**, the exaltation of **Saturn** at twenty-one degrees, the fall of the **Sun** at twenty-nine degrees, and the detriment of **Mars**. It is masculine, diurnal, airy, and "moveable" <term>moveable sign - also known as cardinal, marking the start of a season</term> because the season transitions within it from Summer to Autumn. It is sanguine, masculine, and airy at midday.

# The Sign of Scorpio
Its name in Persian is <insert>Kardam</insert> and in Roman <note>Greek</note> it is *Sephrinos* <note>likely a corruption of *Skorpios*</note>. Its image is that of a scorpion with eight legs: four on the northern side and four on the southern side. The two tips of the scorpion's "horns" were previously considered part of Libra, which borders it. It is the **House of Mars**, the **Fall of the Moon** at its 8th degree <note>traditional astrology usually places the Moon's fall at the 3rd degree of Scorpio</note>, and the **Detriment of Venus**. It is a feminine, nocturnal, watery, southern, and autumnal sign. It is fixed, shortens the day, and rises straight. Its ascension is thirty-six degrees. It is hot in the East, dry in Mars and Jupiter, and in the West their voices are heard. 

It has five **Limits**: Mars, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn. Its three **Faces** are Mars, the Sun, and Venus. Its three **Triplicities** are Venus, Mars, and the Moon. It has degrees belonging to Mars, Jupiter, and the Moon; and other degrees belonging to Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn. It has nine **Ninth-parts**, the first of which is Mars. Its **Lunar Mansions** include two-thirds of the **Crown** <note>original: *al-Iklil*</note>, the **Heart** <note>original: *al-Qalb*</note>, and the **Sting** <note>original: *al-Shawla*</note>. 

Regarding the body, it rules the male organs, the bladder, and the private parts. Its **Fixed Stars** include **Antares** <note>original: *Qalb al-Aqrab*, "the Heart of the Scorpion"</note>, the end of the two northern stars of *al-Fakka*, and *al-Zubana*. Among its degrees that increase happiness are three: the 4th, 18th, and 20th. Its bright degrees are the 6th and 7th. Its dark degree is the 1st. Its "pitted" <note>unlucky</note> degree is the 2nd. Its masculine degrees are three: the 2nd, 4th, and 8th. Its feminine degrees are three: the 3rd, 5th, and 6th. Among the degrees that harm the eyesight are six: the 9th, 10th, 27th, 28th, 23rd, and 28th, which is **Antares**. Its colors range from dusky to red. Its temperament, according to Mars, is coldness and phlegm. It shares characteristics with Aries, Gemini, and Libra, as well as Pisces and Cancer. Regarding the parts of a ship, it represents the mast. In terms of direction, it is the third. Of mountains, the black ones. Of gems, the sharp ones. Of colors, the "fortified" or dark ones. Its characteristic action is **silence**.

# The Sign of Capricorn

Its name in Persian is *Barghak* <note>Vahig</note> and in Greek *Aigokeros* <note>original: *aghujerus*</note>. Its form is that of a goat with two tails and a forward-facing mane, with a curved horn like a horse that has knelt down as if wanting to head toward Syria <note>meaning Northward</note>, and its posture is arched. It is a feminine, nocturnal, southern, wintry sign. It is a **Cardinal** sign <note>original: *munqalib* - marking the start of a season</note> for the turning of time from Autumn into Winter and cold. It is associated with black bile <note>original: *marra sawda*</note>. It has short ascension. The day is longer <note>this likely refers to the days beginning to lengthen after the winter solstice</note>. It is "severed of limbs" and has no voice. Its ascension is twenty-eight degrees. It is a hot sign in the East, agreeing with Jupiter and Mars; in the West, it agrees with the Moon and Venus. It is the **House** of Saturn. Mars is **Exalted** in it at twenty-eight degrees. Jupiter's **Fall** is at fifteen degrees. The **Detriment** of the Moon. It has five **Limits**: Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Mars, and Saturn. Its **Faces** are Jupiter, Mars, and the Sun. Its **Triplicities** are Venus, Mars, and the Moon. It has degrees belonging to Saturn, Venus, and Mercury; and other degrees belonging to the Sun, Mars, and Jupiter. Its nine **Ninth-parts** begin with Saturn. Of the **Lunar Mansions**, it has *Sa'd al-Dhabih* <note>The Lucky One of the Slaughterer</note>, *Sa'd Bula'* <note>The Lucky One of the Devourer</note>, and a third of *Sa'd al-Su'ud* <note>The Luckiest of Lucks</note>. Regarding the body, it rules the lower legs, the <unclear>bitter?</unclear> thigh, and the knees. Its **Fixed Stars** include **Altair** <note>original: *al-Nasr al-Ta'ir*, "the flying eagle"</note>. Among the degrees of fortune: four: the 12th, 14th, 17th, and 20th. Of the bright degrees: two: the 1st and 5th. Of the dark: the 4th. Of the "starry" <note>original: *al-munajjama*</note> degrees: two: the 2nd and 7th. Of the masculine: two: the 12th. Of death: the 8th. Of the "pits" that impair vision: six: the 2nd...

### The Sign of Aquarius
Its name in Persian is *Dul* <note>meaning "the bucket"</note>, and in Greek, it is *Apar-haras* <note>original: *Abhar-haras*, a transliteration of the Greek *Hydrochoos*</note>. Its image is that of a man carrying a vessel, using his hand to pour from it as if he is drawing water from a well. He faces the East. It is the **House** <term>House - the sign where a planet has its primary residence and greatest influence</term> of **Saturn**. The Sun is in its **Detriment** <term>Detriment - the sign opposite a planet's home, where its influence is weakened</term> here; it has no **Exaltation** or **Fall**. It is a masculine, fiery, diurnal, western, dry sign. Its taste is sweet. It is a sign of "crooked ascension" <note>original: *mu’awwaj al-tulu’* - signs that take less time to rise over the horizon</note>. It is also described as feminine and "downward-pouring." Its degree of ascension is twenty-two. It is hot in the East, dry in the West, and elsewhere cold and moist. 

It has five **Limits** <term>Limits (Terms) - subdivisions of a sign ruled by different planets</term>: Mercury, Venus, Jupiter, Mars, and Saturn. Its **Faces** <term>Faces (Decans) - ten-degree subdivisions of a sign</term> are Venus, Mercury, and the Moon. Its **Triplicities** <term>Triplicities - a group of three signs belonging to the same element</term> are Saturn, Mercury, and Jupiter. It has eight degrees belonging to Saturn, Mercury, and Venus. It has three "Smokes" <note>likely referring to *dakhanat*, a less common astrological subdivision used for timing</note>: Saturn, the Moon, and Mercury. It has nine **Ninth-parts**, the first being Saturn. 

Its **Lunar Mansions** <term>Lunar Mansions - 28 divisions of the sky the moon passes through each month</term> are *Sa’d al-Su’ud* <note>The Luck of Lucks</note>, *Sa’d al-Akhbiya* <note>The Luck of the Tents</note>, and a third of *al-Far’ al-Muqaddam* <note>The Front Spout of the Bucket</note>. Its part of the body is the shins. Its stars include those of the Water-Bearer, then Pisces, and *al-Ridf* <note>the star Deneb</note>. Its bright degrees are the 4th, 16th, 17th, and 20th. It also lists degrees that are "veiled," "covered," or "wells" <note>astrological degrees considered unlucky or obstructed</note>, specifically the 1st, 10th, 16th, 23rd, 27th, 29th, and 30th. 

Among colors, it favors yellow and dark yellow. Its nature is cold. It is associated with the signs of the Birds <note>likely referring to the Airy signs</note>, Virgo, Pisces, Gemini, and Libra. Its part of a ship is the sail. Its day is Saturday. Among animals, it rules grey ones. Its gemstone is the ruby. Among colors, it also rules white. Its action is sleep. Regarding its stars: one rises in its 10th degree and 4 minutes; another rises at 14 degrees and 4 minutes at a northern latitude; another rises at 21 degrees and 30 minutes at a northern latitude. The cities and regions it rules include Samarkand, Sogdia, Fergana, Ethiopia, Shash, parts of Egypt, Baghdad, and parts of Qazvin. Its direction is the West, its season is Winter, its month is February <note>original: *Shubat*</note>, and its day is Saturday. Its taste is sweet. ⊙

Its image is that of two fish, the head of one near the tail of the other. They are joined by a cord. It possesses the qualities of the spring season. It is the **House** of **Jupiter**. The **Exaltation** of **Venus** occurs in its twenty-seventh degree, and its **Fall**...

<keywords>Aquarius, Capricorn, Pisces, Saturn, Jupiter, Baghdad, Samarkand, lunar mansions, astrological degrees, zodiac geography</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the zodiac signs, concluding the section on Pisces and its association with the planet Mercury, before transitioning into a new chapter on the fixed stars and constellations of the northern and southern hemispheres.</meta>

Mercury is at fifteen degrees. It is a northern sign <note>Though Pisces is geographically south of the celestial equator, it is often categorized by its seasonal or directional properties in medieval astrology.</note> of a cold nature, and it has "short ascensions" in the West during the equinox. Its rising time <term>Ascension - the time it takes for a sign to rise over the horizon</term> for the arc of its degrees is twenty. It is hot in the East, where it agrees with Mars and Jupiter, but in the West, it opposes them. These are the children of Thursday <note>A reference to Jupiter, the ruler of Thursday</note>. Its **Bounds** <term>Bounds - specific degrees of a sign ruled by different planets</term> belong to Venus, Jupiter, Mercury, Mars, and Saturn. It has three **Faces** <term>Faces/Decans - 10-degree subdivisions of a sign</term> ruled by Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars. Its three **Triplicities** <term>Triplicities - groups of three signs belonging to the same element</term> are Venus, Mars <note>original: *al-Samar* - likely a scribal variant for Mars</note>, and the Moon. Its degrees are assigned to Jupiter, the Sun, and Mars, and its sub-degrees <note>original: *adriyajat*</note> to Venus, the Sun, and the Moon. 

Among the Lunar Mansions <term>Lunar Mansions - 28 divisions of the sky the moon passes through each month</term>, it contains the three parts of the "Front Spout," the "Rear Spout," and the "Belly of the Fish." From the body, it rules the feet. Among the "Gatekeeper" stars, it contains the "Shoulder of the Horse" <note>Markab in Pegasus</note> and the "Head of the Woman" <note>the star Alpheratz in Andromeda</note>. Among the degrees that increase happiness are the 13th and 20th; the "climbing" degrees are the 3rd and 4th; the "canopy" degree is the 2nd; the "encircling" degrees are the 4th and 6th; the "mentioned" degrees are the 8th and 10th; and the feminine degrees are the 3rd and 13th. 

There are six "Burnt Degrees" <note>original: *al-muhtariqa* - degrees considered harmful or weakened</note>: the 3rd, 9th, 21st, 24th, 17th, and 28th. Its color is tawny or dusty. Its nature is characterized by heat and dryness. Among the signs, it is associated with Leo, the Animal <note>possibly Lupus or a specific lunar mansion</note>, and Aquarius; and beneath it are Cancer and Scorpio. From a ship, it represents the oars; from the Imam <note>possibly a reference to a chess piece or a specific social role</note>, the fifth; from horses, the black ones <note>original: *al-daham*</note>; from gems, aquamarine; from plants, the rough ones; and from actions, grief. The "Gatekeeper" stars rise from it at approximately five degrees and seven minutes, with a northern latitude. Among the lands and cities, it rules Sarry, Luwayn, Lakda, Lufa, and Khuzma. Its season is winter and its month is March. 

There are seven images associated with these signs which have movements, stations, and effects on those born under them, as well as crafts which we have omitted from this book of ours. However, we have mentioned them exhaustively with thoroughness and diligent description in the book we titled ***Al-Muhit*** <note>original: *The Encompassing* or *The Comprehensive Book*</note>. Whoever wishes to attain the necessary knowledge regarding the signs as established, written, and classified by the scholars, let them seek—by God's will and aid—our book *Al-Muhit*. God is the Granter of success through His grace.

## Chapter Three: On the Images of the Northern and Southern Constellations and Their States

We begin, by God's will and aid, by mentioning the northern constellations, God willing.

**The Little Bear** <note>Ursa Minor</note>: Its stars are seven. Its image is that of a bear facing the Pole. There are no stars in its head or body except for the four stars in its tail <note>original: *al-liyya* - the fatty tail of a sheep, used here metaphorically</note> and the four stars in its middle.

**The Great Bear** <note>Ursa Major</note>: Its stars are seventeen <note>While usually cited as 27 or more stars, this manuscript specifies 17</note>. Its image is that of a bear; its face, head, and body are directed toward the celestial equator. From its head comes the tail.

**The Dragon** <note>Draco</note>: Its stars are thirty-one. Behind it is a coil of its width, extending like a line. If its neck were turned toward its head, it would be larger; it stretches from the back of the Great Bear to the "Mother Camels" <note>original: *al-Awa'idh* - a group of stars in the head of Draco</note>, which are four stars. The "Mother Camels" constitute the head of the Dragon. Six stars, and a fifth one in the "Pasture of the Mother Camels," are at the head of the Dragon. The stars closest to the Pole are the small ones on the back of the Dragon, shaped like a triangle.

**The Inflamed** <note>Cepheus</note>: Also called **Qiqawus** <note>original: *Qiqawus* - a transliteration of the Greek *Kepheus*</note>. Its stars are eleven. Its image is that of a man wearing a cap, sitting with one leg raised and the other placed down. His head is toward the celestial equator, and his feet are in front of the "Trotting Camel" <note>original: *al-Wazb*</note>. 

Before him is **Boqtas** <note>original: *Boqtas* - a corruption of the Greek *Boötes*</note>, which is **The Shouter** <note>original: *al-Awwa*</note>, meaning "The Lamp" or "The Ghoul."

<summary>This page concludes the astrological properties of Pisces and Mercury, referencing the author's more comprehensive work, *Al-Muhit*, before beginning a catalog of northern constellations including Ursa Major, Ursa Minor, Draco, and Cepheus.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, Pisces, Al-Muhit, Ursa Minor, Ursa Major, Draco, Cepheus, Boötes, Northern Constellations, Astrology, Astronomy</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the discussion of the zodiac signs (ending with Pisces) into a detailed catalogue of the northern constellations. It follows the classical tradition of describing the fixed stars by their mythical figures, positions relative to the celestial pole, and the number of stars they contain.</meta>

**The Ghoul** <note>original: *al-Ghul* — usually referring to Perseus, though the "Ghoul" specifically refers to the star Algol</note> who guards the heavens and is the mightiest; its stars are twenty-two in number. His image is that of a man whose head is toward the direction of the Pole, placing one of his hands on the path of the Milky Way, which is near the Pole. He appears to be falling upon his feet, though he is of sound form, as if he were a courtier in the mine of the northern night and wind.

**The Crown** which is the **Broken Ring** <note>original: *al-Fakka* — Corona Borealis</note>; its stars are eight, and its image is that of a ring.

**The Kneeler** <note>original: *al-Jathi* — Hercules</note>; its stars are twenty-eight. His image is that of a man kneeling on his knees, his sword raised high. His head is toward the direction of the equator, and his feet are toward the North Pole.

**The Lute** <note>original: *al-Lawza* — Lyra</note>, which is the **Falling Eagle**, also called the **Turtle** <note>The Greek name *Lyra* comes from the myth of Hermes making a lyre out of a tortoise shell</note>. Its stars are ten, and its image is that of a bird wearing a rounded hood on its head. Its face is toward the North Pole, and its wings are spread toward the equator.

**The Hen** <note>original: *al-Dajaja* — Cygnus, the Swan</note>; its stars are seventeen. Its image is that of a duck with a wide, broad beak. At the end of its range, in its beak, is a bright star <note>Albireo</note> located on the path of the Milky Way. Its head is toward the equator and the East; one wing is toward the North Pole and the other toward the equator. It has two legs, claws, and a tail.

**The Lady in the Chair** <note>original: *Dhat al-Kursi* — Cassiopeia</note>, the "Stained One," and her stars are thirteen. Her image is that of a woman sitting on a chair. From the hand of her finery <unclear>...</unclear> the chair is behind the chair, its legs and corners are toward the equator, and her feet are toward the North Pole. <gloss>Pashawush</gloss> <note>A variant name, likely Persian in origin, for Perseus</note>

**The Head of the Ghoul** <note>The Medusa head held by Perseus</note> which is the **Bearer**; its stars are twenty-six. Its image is that of a man riding a horse <note>A curious historical variation; Perseus is usually depicted walking, though associated with Pegasus</note> holding the head of a ghoul in his hand. The head resembles a mass, its surface has passed, and the man’s face is toward the equator while the Ghoul’s head is toward the East.

**The Charioteer** <note>original: *al-Ayyuq* — Capella/Auriga</note>, he who holds the reins. The Romans call it "The Sorrow," and its stars are fourteen. Its image is that of a human holding horse reins in his hand, his face toward the equator. On his head is a helmet shaped like an egg with a tail six cubits long.

**The Serpent Bearer** <note>original: *al-Hawwa* — Ophiuchus</note>; twenty-four stars. His image is that of a man whose head is toward the North Pole and his feet toward the equator. He holds the middle of the serpent straight from both sides.

**The Serpent of the Bearer** <note>original: *Hayyat al-Hawwa* — Serpens</note>; its stars are eighteen. Its image is that of a serpent whose head is toward the Pole and its tail is toward the East.

**The Goat** <note>Likely a reference to a star near Lyra or a misidentification here</note>, which is the **Foolish One**; its stars are five. Its head is in the ring on the <unclear>...</unclear>. Some people call it **The Arrow** <note>Sagitta</note>, which is the image of an archer’s arrow with feathers and a point. The foot of the Eagle <note>Aquila</note> is a bird above it, as if landing on it. The head of the arrow is toward the East and its notch toward the West.

<margin>The elements the s<unclear>...</unclear></margin>

**The Eagle** which is the **Flying Eagle** <note>original: *al-Nasr al-Ta'ir* — Altair/Aquila</note>. Its image is that of a bird with a beak like those of eagles, and its legs have the claws of eagles, though it is a "false" eagle. Its wings are spread, its head is toward the equator, and its claws are upon the Arrow. Its head is toward the East and its face toward the West, low in the Pole. Its stars are nine.

**The Dolphin** <note>Delphinus</note>; its stars are fourteen. It is the "Beast of the Serpent" <note>The text uses a strange phrase here, *Sab' al-Hayyah*</note>. Its image is that of a sea beast; its head is like the head of a beast of prey, but its tail is that of a fish. It is seen from its side. Its tail is like a fish’s tail, and its head is toward the North Pole.

**The First Horse** <note>Equuleus</note>; its stars are four, standing out. Its image is the head of a horse. The Arabs call it the "Head of the Camel." It is open-mouthed, facing the North Pole.

**The Second Horse** <note>Pegasus</note>; its stars are twenty. Its image is a winged horse, its head toward the North Pole, facing it. One wing is in front and the other behind toward the equator. Its front half is in Aquarius and its rear half is with the stars of Aquarius that are in Pisces.

**The Woman Who Has Not Seen a Husband** <note>original: *al-Mar’a al-latī lam tara ba’lan* — Andromeda</note>; its stars are twenty-two. Her image is a woman with two braids, sitting with her hands and limbs before her. From her comes this side, which is the shining star on the chain <note>Mirach</note> above her waist. Her head is toward the West and her feet toward the East.

**The Triangle** <note>Triangulum</note>; four stars, and its image is that of a ringed shape.
