# Names of the Southern Constellations

**The Sea Monster** <note>original: *Qaytus* — Cetus</note>, also known as the Beast of the Sea. Its stars are twenty-two in number. Its image is situated behind the Bull <note>Taurus</note>; it has two ears shaped like wells, a tongue, and a head. It possesses an ear, a shoulder, and a mane. Its head is positioned low in the belly toward the south, extending toward the east. Its ear is aligned with the middle of the Fish <note>Pisces</note>. This is clearly visible from **The Giant** <note>original: *al-Jabbar* — Orion</note>.

**The Giant** <note>Orion</note>; its stars are twenty-eight in number. Its image is that of a man standing and facing the prayer direction <note>original: *mustaqbil al-qibla* — facing south/forward</note>, with a sword hanging from him. In one of his hands, he holds a bird-like object resembling a skin, with the hand extended toward the east. In the other hand is a curved object <note>a shield or skin</note>. Above this, Ptolemy named it a "shield," meaning the curved object in his hand. One of his legs reaches near the tail of the Southern Dog, while his other leg is upon the head of the River. The star Al-Mirzam <note>Bellatrix</note> is by his legs.

**The River** <note>original: *al-Nahr* — Eridanus</note>; its stars are thirty-four in number. Its image is that of a snake-like winding form starting from the western foot of Orion <note>the star Rigel</note>, which is not particularly bright, reaching to the middle of the body. It then bends toward the middle of the hip. The River resembles a fish that is joined to the Ram <note>Aries</note> from the direction of the Fish <note>Pisces</note>.

**The Hare** <note>original: *al-Arnab* — Lepus</note>; its stars are twelve in number. Its image is that of a hare located beneath the foot of the Giant <note>Orion</note> which contains the stars of Al-Mirzam.

**The Lesser Dog** <note>original: *al-Kalb al-Asghar* — Canis Minor</note>; its stars are two in number. It is located behind the beginning of the sign of Cancer, and the rest of its body is within Cancer. Its name is associated with its tail in the latitude of Cancer. It stands behind the [Greater] Dog, and the rest of its body is low in the great southern region. In its neck is the star *al-Shi‘ra al-Ghumaisa* <note>Procyon</note>, also known as the Syrian Star. In its [description] at the passage of the tail is the bright northern star.

**The Greater Dog** <note>original: *al-Kalb al-Akbar* — Canis Major</note>; its stars are eighteen in number. Its image is that of a dog standing at the beginning of Cancer, low in the south. Half of its body is in Cancer. Within it is the star *al-Shi‘ra al-‘Abur* <note>Sirius</note>, which is the Yemenite Star.

**The Ship** <note>original: *al-Safina* — Argo Navis</note>; its stars are forty-five in number. Its image is that of a ship with a rudder, an oar, a sail, and a mast. It begins from the twenty-first degree of Cancer to the twenty-first degree of Virgo. The bottom of the ship is in the deep south, while the light of its prow and its lower part are below the equator. The star *Suhail* <note>Canopus</note> is at the tip of the rudder.

**The Water Snake** <note>original: *al-Shuja‘* — Hydra</note>; its stars are twenty-five in number. Its image is that of a serpent beginning from the middle of Cancer and reaching to the tenth degree of Libra, extending toward the head of the Riding Man <note>Centaurus</note> who holds a falcon and a spear in his hand, which he has turned toward the leg of the Wild Beast <note>Lupus</note>. The tail of the Hydra is placed upon the head of the Wild Ass <note>perhaps referring to stars in Leo or Virgo</note>. The face of the snake's back is toward the north, its head toward the South Pole, its face toward the east, and its tail toward the west.

**The Cup** <note>original: *al-Kas* — Crater</note>; its stars are seven. Its image is placed upon the dominant ribs [of the Hydra]. Its top and bottom are double-layered. The bottom of the Cup is inside the body of the Water Snake. The top of the Cup is toward the west, beneath the "pincers" of the Scorpion <note>referring to the general region of Libra/Scorpio</note>, and its bottom is toward the South Pole. Its image is curved on a level plane. The Cup begins at the eighteenth degree of Virgo, with its head overturned toward its bottom and upward.

**The Crow** <note>original: *al-Ghurab* — Corvus</note>; its stars are six. Its image is that of a crow pecking with its beak, showing its head and two wings. The beginning of the beak is near the Water Snake, and the tip of its tail is at the first of the emerging lines. Its face and leg are toward the east, while its back and the nape of its neck are toward the south, positioned crosswise rather than standing.

**The Centaur** <note>original: *Qanturus* — Centaurus</note>; its stars are thirty-seven. Its image is that of a man upon a horse. In his hand is a falcon, and he holds the reins. He advances toward the hand of the Wild Beast <note>Lupus</note>. Between his hands, the hand touching the leg of the Beast makes it appear as if he wishes to [unclear] for him. His face is toward the east and he...

**The Wild Beast** <note>original: *al-Sab‘* — the constellation Lupus, often depicted as a generic predatory animal</note>; its stars are nineteen in number. Its image is that of a leopard. Its ear points toward the east, its head toward the equator, and the tip of its tail toward the South Pole at the furthest point of its inclination. It is tilted between the north and the south, and those stars with the greatest latitude are at its tip.

**The Censer** <note>original: *al-Mijmar* — the constellation Ara, the Altar</note>; its stars are seven. Its head is toward the South Pole, slightly inclined toward the west. Its legs point toward the equator, slightly tilted. The ends of its legs are at seven degrees of the Archer <note>Sagittarius</note>. It has three legs, one of which is joined to the fourth joint of the tail of the Scorpion.

**The Southern Crown** <note>original: *al-Iklil al-Janubi* — Corona Australis</note>; its stars are thirteen in number. Its image is a round shape with two paths <note>or "legs"</note> facing toward the west. It is situated in the middle of the legs of the Censer. The Crown is between the legs of the Horse <note>Centaurus</note>, appearing as a circle. Behind it, the Crown is doubled and twisted, with its rings overlapping.

The Crown consists of thirteen marked stars in the Archer.

**The Southern Fish** <note>original: *al-Hut al-Janubi* — Piscis Austrinus</note>; its head is toward the west, and the tip of its fin <note>or "wing"</note> is above the tail toward the east. Its tail is toward the South Pole. In its middle is a slight bend. Its tail extends from twenty-five degrees of the Goat <note>Capricorn</note> to eighteen degrees of the Water-Bearer <note>Aquarius</note>. Its tail has been beautifully elevated. Behind it is a fish with a spine on its back, a fin, and a tail. Its stars are eleven in number. Thus, the southern stars [constellations] total twenty-eight.

->**The Conditions of the Stars in Magnitude**<-
<note>In medieval astronomy, stars were categorized into six "magnitudes" based on their apparent size and brightness. These were also used to estimate the physical volume of the stars relative to the Earth.</note>

Among them, in the **first magnitude**, there are fifteen stars; each of these is one hundred and seven times the size of the Earth. In the **second magnitude**, there are forty-five stars; each of these stars is ninety times the size of the Earth. In the **third magnitude**, there are [unclear] stars; each of these stars is seventy-two times the size of the Earth. In the **fourth magnitude**, there are seventy-four stars; each of these is fifty-four times the size of the Earth. In the **fifth magnitude**, there are one hundred and seventeen stars; each star is thirty-six times the size of the Earth. In the **sixth magnitude**, there are sixty-three stars; each of these stars is [unclear] <note>The text here is difficult to read; traditionally, the 6th magnitude was cited as 18 times the size of the Earth</note> times the size of the Earth.

**The stars seen by Joseph** the Prophet—peace be upon him—in his dream as they moved, not by the striking of winds: The Rider of Amuran, the Splitter of the Dawn, the One of the Emptying <note>or "The Spout"</note>, the Clouds <note>or "Covenant"</note>, the One of the Two Palms, the Thirsty One, the Atmosphere of the Sun, and the Solitary Ones.

->**The Fourth Chapter: On Knowing the Refinement of the Stars and the Reality of Actions**<-

The Persians and the Indians have also mentioned the indications of their specific properties from the "Five Pillars" <note>likely the five visible planets: Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note>. When they follow in the degrees of the Ascendants, the nativities, the consequences, or the midheaven, or in the degrees of the Illuminaries <note>the Sun and Moon</note>, this increases knowledge according to how they correspond with the auspicious and inauspicious portents. We have mentioned them and their names in Persian in a summarized manner, God the Exalted willing.

<meta>This page transitions from the physical description of constellations and stars on page 12 to the judicial astrology of comets. It establishes a link between celestial phenomena and earthly disasters based on the location of these "long-haired stars" within the zodiac.</meta>

# Chapter Six: On the Conditions of Comets and the Wonders Within Them

<margin>In the world</margin>
The sages have stated that the essence of the omens associated with comets <note>original: *al-kawakib dhawat al-dhawayib*, literally "stars with forelocks" or "tressed stars"</note> resembles the omens of Mars and Mercury when their influences are combined. If someone sees a comet in the celestial sphere where the rays of these two planets have mixed, it indicates conquest, wars, killings, fires <note>original: *niyzan*, likely a misspelling of *niran* (fires)</note>, plagues, the cutting off of agricultural provisions, an abundance of pestilence, earthquakes, and eclipses <note>original: *husuf*, likely referring to *khasf* (earthquakes/land subsidence) or *khusuf* (lunar eclipses)</note>. It also foretells the death of scholars and kings. These events will occur in the direction where the comet appeared; if it appears in the East, then in the East; if in the West, then in the West; if in the North, then in the North. If it appears in the South, the affliction will be universal throughout the world and the sphere in the center of the region. If its exaltation is derived from the Sun, its omens are hastened, but if it is occidental <note>setting after the sun</note>, its omens are delayed. If it is not <margin>no</margin> near [to the Sun], its omens are few. If it remains visible for a long duration, its effects will be long-lasting. The region toward which the tail tilts is the one it shall drive its influence toward. And God knows best his own unseen secrets.

## Discourse on Their Appearance in Each Zodiac Sign

<margin>Rain</margin>
If a comet appears in the sign of **Aries**, the great and the noble shall rule, while the foolish and the lowly shall be enslaved by unknown evils. The King of the Romans <note>referring to the Byzantine Emperor</note> and the dignitaries of his kingdom will die, his lands will fall into turmoil, and destruction will increase. A sharp sword [war] and famine due to lack of rain and pasture shall afflict the land of the Turks. Eye pains will become frequent, the summer heat will intensify, and gold and silver will increase in the hands of the people. 

If it appears in the sign of **Taurus**, the cold will become difficult and harsh, ruining the grass. People will suffer severe ailments such as coughs, dryness, itchiness, and scabies. Cattle will die, roads will be lost, and harvests will be spoiled, afflicted by blight in both the plains and the mountains. Water will be scarce in the mountains, and the water of gardens and paths will diminish. The farmers <note>original: *al-akkaraun*</note> and the people of the rural lands <note>original: *ahl al-sawad*, specifically the fertile lands of Iraq</note> will perish from oppression, tyranny, and a lack of safety. 

If it appears in **Gemini**, poisonous winds <note>original: *al-samam*</note> will burn the fruit and the wilderness. Physicians will perish from the intensity of the heat, plague will fall upon the people, and children will die. Mountains will collapse, shooting stars will appear, and great lightning will race across the sky with terrifying sounds like thunder, appearing as flames and intense flashes.

If it appears in the sign of **Cancer**, people will be hit by heavy, destructive rain that destroys houses. Rivers will overflow, springs will flood, and homes will be submerged. Many creatures will die from drowning or choking. Cities and villages will perish, wars will increase, and the world will be terrified in both inland cities and islands. Roads will be cut off in the regions of Armenia, Khorasan, and the lands of Persia due to excessive mud, and disputes and wars will break out among the people over water.

If it appears in the sign of **Leo**, kings will die at the hands of one another, wars will break out, and sickness and fever will intensify in the direction of the East. One of the greatest and most significant kings will die. A deep redness will appear in the sky, and winds will stir that carry dust, level hills, and uproot trees. Fear will enter the homes, grain will be eaten [by pests], and people will suffer from constipation and stomach pains.

If it appears in **Virgo**, there will be a greatness in the crops followed by a decline, dryness, and severe drought in the land. Men will suffer severe pains in their bowels. When it passes toward the south <note>original: *al-tyasa*, likely a reference to the southern direction or a specific star group</note>, there will be pains in the wombs of women, and the people will experience terrors, agitation, death, and ailments, along with the greatness of oppression from the common folk and the lowly. 

If it appears in **Libra**, fruits will be spoiled by a blight that enters them, winds will intensify and turn into gales, some of the kings of that region will die, and oppression, tyranny, and heavy rains will increase.

<summary>This page details the astrological omens of comets, explaining how their appearance in specific zodiac signs predicts natural disasters, political upheaval, and widespread disease. The text links celestial movement directly to the fate of kings, farmers, and various geographical regions.</summary>

<keywords>comets, astrology, zodiac signs, Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, omens, drought, Byzantine Empire, plague, meteorology</keywords>

--- Page 27 ---
<meta>This page continues the prognostications for comets appearing in the remaining signs of the zodiac, beginning with the conclusion of the previous sign's effects and moving through Scorpius to Pisces. It then transitions into a new section citing Ptolemy regarding the specific physical types of comets and their unique influences.</meta>

...people will be healthy and diseases will decrease, but dryness will increase in the land and its grass will wither. The affairs of the people and their livelihoods will be set right, though their ruler shall suffer losses.

If a comet appears in the sign of **Scorpius**, there will be many clouds in that year, the sky will be humid, and springs will be abundant. There will be great pride <note>original: *zawa*, likely *zahuw* (arrogance or splendor)</note> and much bloodshed in the North. In the West, there will be war and a resounding defeat. The cold will be severe, and death, ailments, and killings will multiply. There will be a prevalence of licentiousness, and children will appear aged <note>original: *sib al-wildan*, referring to hair turning grey prematurely from trauma</note>; this will result from the influence of women over men <note>original: *istatbab al-nisa*, suggesting a reversal of traditional social roles</note>. Men will be afflicted with many pains in the genitals, the bladder <note>original: *al-matana*, a misspelling of *al-mathana*</note>, and the stomach. Rains will recur that are harmful to fruits, the yield of milk will increase, and the cold and frost <note>original: *al-karaz*</note> will intensify.

If it appears in the sign of **Sagittarius**, the air will be stifling <note>original: *sahar*, likely meaning parched or magically altered</note>, the heat will intensify, and the beasts of the wilderness will die. This will stir up hot, pestilential winds <note>original: *samum*, the "poison wind" of the desert</note> that bring disease. Signs will be seen in the sky among the stars, and they shall be contradictory. The King will be harsh toward both the commoners and the elite, being greedy in amassing wealth. Men will be humiliated and driven to despair by coercion, injustice, and oppression. A great man among the King’s enemies and warriors will die in the East of that region.

If it appears in **Capricornus**, there will be intense terror and war of great magnitude. Certain heresies <note>original: *bid'a*, innovations in religious practice</note> will appear, and people will be driven to states resembling madness, obsessive whispering <note>original: *waswas*</note>, and epilepsy. Opinions will be corrupted. There will be distress and hardship in livelihoods and the affliction of hot winds. Later, the air will turn cold, the growth of crops will diminish, and young goats will die. People will make earnest pleas to the heavens because of the stars that appear therein. The roads will become perilous, thieves will multiply, and those known for piety, righteousness, and godliness will be humiliated. People will increase in their oppression of one another.

If it appears in the sign of **Aquarius**, there will be death among the people, great hardship, plague, and killing. The price of everything—fruits, oil, juice, and grains—will rise. People will seek refuge with a leader from among the Blacks <note>original: *al-suda*, likely referring to people of East African or Sudanese origin</note>. A king from the kings of the East will die. There will be much internal strife and disagreement. A loud cry will be heard that causes many people to die, and there will be fear from the frequency of thunder, hail, and the terror of lightning strikes. Drought will persist throughout the years. ⊙

If it appears in the sign of **Pisces**, a great nation from the people of the South and the East of the earth will take power. They will introduce a religious practice <note>original: *sunna*</note> that was previously unknown and contrary to established Law <note>original: *al-shar'*</note>. Sovereignty will pass from one community to another. The lowly among the people will confront the pious and God-fearing. Conflict will arise regarding the waters <note>original: *al-mayir*, likely *al-miyah*</note>, and the poor will manifest their destitution out of fear of misfortune. Deceit, treachery, and malice will become common. Scholars <note>original: *al-ahbar*, often referring to Jewish or Christian learned men</note> will die. Birds will not hatch, and the young will perish. People will be ruined by the death of fish, perhaps due to the drying up of waters <note>original: *al-jafna*</note>. And God knows best the truth of the matter. ⊙

->**What Ptolemy narrated regarding the actions of comets individually and their specific indications**<-

He said: When the one named **"Patro"** <note>original: *Batru*, possibly a corruption of a Greek name like *Pitar* or *Veru*</note> appears—which is associated with the nature of Venus, though it is distinguished by a judgment different from hers—it indicates a greatness of purpose when it is full. It is fast-moving and possesses a mane like that of a horse, casting its rays behind it. It travels through the twelve signs of the zodiac. When it appears, it causes distress to kings and tyrants, and many great and momentous events occur in the location toward which its tail inclines. Its nature does not entirely match that of Venus; rather, they intended to liken its color and nature to her by association. This is its form: ⊙

<image-desc>A drawing of a dark, curved celestial body resembling a thick crescent or a comet's tail pointing downwards.</image-desc>

<margin>
The Horse's Mane
</margin>

He said: Regarding the one named **"The Warlike"** <note>original: *al-Harbiyya*</note>, its color gleams with rays of yellow and black. It is born with a head like a lizard <note>original: *al-sawah*</note>. When it appears in the East, it indicates the killing of the kings' enemies in the West. If it is in the East [it affects the West], and if it is in the West, it affects the East. It makes the drawing of swords permissible. It brings heavy rains, winds, and leaning omens in the sky. This is its form: ⊙

<margin>The Lamp</margin>
If that which is named **The Lamp** appears—which is <term>Mars</term> <note>original: *al-Marrikh*</note>—it is an elongated star with a red shoulder for which there is no remedy. If it appears in the East, it indicates a severe drought in the regions, as well as locusts, distress <note>original: *al-ghassa*, perhaps meaning "choking" or social strife</note>, the shedding of blood, and a multitude of lightning strikes in the land of <unclear>Taha</unclear> <note>This may refer to a specific region or be a scribal error</note>, the origin of which is unknown to those who stay awake at night, the marshes, or the winds. It foretells the burning of what the kings have prepared for themselves, especially in the heat of the day, the drying up of springs and rivers, an increase in hardship across the horizons, and a frequency of falling stars and lightning. If it appears in the West or the South, it indicates sedition occurring in the middle of the region where it is seen, along with many blockades, high prices, war, and the corruption of crops in the West. This is its form: ۝

<margin>The Bearded One</margin>
When the star known as **The Bearded One** <note>original: *al-Lihani*</note> appears—which belongs to <term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: *al-Mushtari*</note>—it has a beautiful ray like a flash of lightning, colored a yellowish-white like silver. When it moves, it dazzles. It has a period of stillness, and its "shoulder" is soft to the human eye, which cannot endure its light. When a person perceives it, it brings goodness. It rises after the eleventh <note>likely the 11th hour or 11th day</note> with these characteristics, casting its rays. This calls for locusts <note>the text here is ambiguous; it may imply a warning against them</note> but also fertility for the district and the region to which it pertains, especially if Jupiter is in **Pisces**, **Cancer**, or **Scorpio**. In such cases, prosperity will come easily, people will be generous in their livelihoods, and it will benefit the nobility and those of high lineage. People will learn of the love that the kings have for their subjects, justice will be manifest among them, and winds will be frequent. This is its form: ۝

<margin><unclear>The Fasi</unclear></margin>
If that which is called **The <unclear>Fasi</unclear>** <note>The name is partially obscured but likely refers to a specific type of comet associated with Saturn</note> appears—which belongs to <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: *Zuhal*</note>—its color is like that of Saturn: small and black. It is circular and has a great revolving ray. It remains for a long time in its orbit. When it appears, it stirs up fighting, raids, and wars in every land. The death of great kings will occur—the death of one of the world's greatest rulers—along with the displacement of people and their retinues. Humiliation will fall upon the people of the world, the cities, and the estates. It brings gloom, negligence, and affects those of professions and crafts. This is its form: ۝

<margin>The Rosy One</margin>
When **The Rosy Star** <note>original: *al-Mawrid*, meaning "rosy" or "the arriving one"</note> appears, it possesses a brilliant light. Its color is that of gold and tin when they are mixed. It is grand and is called the "Star of the Warrior and the Subjects." It has a face like a human face because of its height, and its rays emanate from the position of the head. It is pleasant to look upon, and the eye finds its beauty gentle. When it appears, it indicates the destruction of the great, but the changing of affairs into something better. It brings the release of those in prisons and the freeing of captives from the Land of the Romans <note>Byzantium</note>, as well as the improvement of the subjects' affairs and security in the cities, deserts, and wildernesses. This is its form: ۝

<margin>Tayfur</margin>
If the star known as **Tayfur** appears—which is grand in appearance, round, and of a reddish-black color—it is "clipped" <note>original: *maqsus*, perhaps referring to a short or blunt tail</note> and possesses a flame and six <unclear>tails</unclear>. It has no companion and is slow-moving. It has "chambers" for the valleys and is warned of after years in the northern heat. When it appears, it indicates the migration of people from their districts, a rise in prices, the corruption of the day, and the destruction of the lower classes, thieves, and slanderers. It also affects all venomous beasts and those with evil claws, such as lions and <unclear>predators</unclear>. When it carries in its heart lightning like thunderbolts, it harms women, destroys cattle, and causes prices to rise. In the mines, those who deal in drugs <note>medicinal or chemical substances</note> will find success, but the King of the East will perish, as will the Arabs. This is its form: ۝

<margin>The Living One</margin>
When the star named **The Living One** <note>original: *al-Hayy*</note> and **The Bearded One** <note>original: *al-Lihani*; this name refers to the "beard" or coma of a comet</note> appears, it only shows itself in the northern signs of the <term>Zodiac</term> <note>original: *al-buruj*</note>. These two stars indicate drought, a lack of rainfall, and the corruption or devaluation of the <term>dinar</term> <note>the gold currency of the time</note>. This is its form:

<margin>The Auspicious Ones</margin>
When the **Auspicious Ones** <note>original: *al-Su‘ud*</note> appear, it is... <note>text lost due to damage</note>. It is small and round, tending toward a very clear red color, appearing late and being elongated but without a distinct "touch" or texture. Its beginning is like a blaze on the head of something smaller than it. It has a bend in its middle, a clear color, and a weak presence. It possesses a circular substance. Its rising brings "poisonous winds" <note>original: *samum*; hot, dry desert winds</note> and it appears in all four quarters of the year, bringing both extreme heat and extreme cold. The sages have classified it among the "Long Red" types of people <note>likely referring to its astrological correspondence to certain human temperaments</note>. This is its form: ٥

When the **Sensory One** <note>original: *al-Hassa*</note> rises—which is a star shaped exactly like a sense organ—if it is one of the "living" stars that are sensory, its influence occurs exactly upon the position where it appears. This is its form: ٥

<margin>The Deception</margin>
As for the round red star whose blackness is dark, the people of India say they see it among them and call it **Al-Kayd** <note>In medieval Islamic astrology, Al-Kayd was an "imaginary" dark planet or comet, often considered the most malevolent of all celestial bodies</note>. It rises upon them like a great betrayal. It is among the most ominous, diminishing, and destructive of stars regarding one's needs. Indeed, the ancient nations that perished—such as the people of **‘Ad**, **Thamud**, the people of **Midian**, and the people of **Lot** <note>groups mentioned in the Qur'an and regional tradition as having been destroyed by divine wrath for their sins</note>—were destroyed at its rising. Because of their haste, they met a similar destruction. It is more sinister than the conjunction of <term>Mars</term> <note>original: *al-Marrikh*</note> and <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: *Zuhal*</note>. This is its form: ٥

## Chapter Seven: On the hidden stars with notched spears in the Ninth Sphere, which act for good and evil

<note>The "Ninth Sphere" in medieval cosmology was the highest heaven, often called the Crystalline Sphere, which moved all the others</note>

Among them is a star called **Hawash**, appearing in this likeness: <image-desc>A cluster of five red dots arranged in a pentagon shape.</image-desc> And the star of his son, **Butlam Fransis** <note>original: *Butlam Fransis*; likely a corrupted transliteration of a Greek or Latin name, possibly related to Ptolemy</note>, appearing with the power of eternal thunder, in this likeness: <image-desc>A cluster of five red dots arranged in a vertical cross-like shape.</image-desc> And two small stars between them... from the signs near the "Whirl" <note>original: *al-falkah*</note>, and their names in <term>Greek</term> <note>original: *al-Rumiyya*</note> are different from this example: <image-desc>Two small red dots side-by-side.</image-desc> And a yellow star within the red, a "sweet" one south of its son <unclear>also</unclear> from above in the Sensory Dog-Star <note>original: *al-Shi‘ri al-Hassa*; likely referring to Sirius</note>, and this is its form: ٥ <image-desc>A single red dot.</image-desc>

And two stars in front of **The Stained Hand** <note>original: *Kaff al-Khadib*; the star Beta Cassiopeiae</note>, one of them closer than the other, so that they are known as "The Abundance" <note>original: *al-Khadam*</note>. Their names are **Harmil Lakla**, and they seek beauty. This is their form: <image-desc>Two red dots.</image-desc> And the star **Shar Shams**... these are small stars surrounding **The Shouter** <note>original: *al-Na‘iz*; likely the star Capella</note>, just as **Hermes** <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy and astrology</note> named it, along with those around it called "The Screamers." The Romans call them **Khwazish**. Its presence is generally in the four seasons, and they said its year is a solar year. When it cuts through the sphere over ages and cycles, it first descends into **Aries** <note>original: *al-Hamal*</note>. For the inhabitants of Aries, if one were born in the first "face" <note>a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</note> of Aries, there would follow for him relief, joy, great prestige, and a kingdom. Kings would frequent him. This is its form: ٥ <image-desc>A cluster of seven red dots.</image-desc>

And two black stars that rise and travel through the middle of the atmosphere quickly, exiting quickly, resembling **Ptolemy** <note>original: *Butlanos*</note> and **Mercury** <note>original: *‘Atarid*</note>. He called it **The Affectionate** <note>original: *al-‘Atuf*</note>. When it descends into the sign of <unclear>the sign</unclear>, it gives a single cry and bequeaths... <note>text lost due to damage at the bottom of the page</note>... for the Sultan so that he does not fear, and they both encompass... in every sign in three hundred and eight hundred faces from Mars to it, and there is no recompense...

<meta>This page continues the astrological catalog of stars and comets, following the description of "The Sensory One." It introduces "The Compassionate One" and several other stars associated with the teachings of Hermes Trismegistus and Ptolemy, detailing their movements, visual appearances, and their effects on human health and nature.</meta>

It is named **The Compassionate One** <note>original: *al-‘Atuf*; likely referring to its gentle influence or a specific curvature in its path</note> because it causes no harm in its passing, yet it brings evil during its retrograde motion. Its image is <image-desc>four red dots arranged horizontally</image-desc>. There is another star followed by six bright, luminous "sailors" <note>original: *mallah*; a term used for stars that seem to "navigate" the sky</note> positioned upon the "bones of the serpents," some of which form a crooked line of six. Hermes named it **The Clear One** <note>original: *al-Yasih*</note> and Ptolemy named it **The Galleons** <note>original: *al-Bawarij*</note>.

It is fortunate and auspiciously blessed. It rises once every forty years, traveling near the path of Jupiter <note>original: *al-Mushtari*</note>, and its temperament is close to that of Jupiter. When it enters a sign, it brings the ornament of blessing, happiness, or evil depending on the ruler of that sign. Its image is <image-desc>seven red dots arranged in a constellation pattern</image-desc>.

There are also two northern stars <note>possibly *al-Muzda*</note>, though they are on the edge of the "Great Path" above the Milky Way and the zenith of the sphere. Looking at them in Leo <note>original: *al-Asad*</note>, they are two unlucky ones among the prominent stars. Hermes named them **The Fearful Ones** <note>original: *al-Matru‘a*</note> and Ptolemy named them **The Tall Ones** <note>original: *Tarmahin*</note>. They enter a sign every twenty-one years. Their image is <image-desc>three red dots in a triangle</image-desc>.

There is also the star **The Restrained One** <note>original: *Kabha*</note> which rises from the link in the path of Canopus <note>original: *Suhayl*</note> and appears at the farthest point of the orbital path. Their image is <image-desc>three red dots in a triangle</image-desc>. In their path are also two "shrouded" stars <note>original: *maghfara*; referring to a dim or hazy appearance</note>. If they appear dark, it indicates intensity, for they are on this broken ring as if they were circles of flowing locks. They cross the celestial sphere once every eighteen years.

Hermes named them **The Rising Ones** <note>original: *al-Tawali‘*</note>, and they are auspicious for all the Zodiac signs. Whoever looks at them at the beginning of the year will not suffer from ophthalmia <note>eye inflammation</note> during that year, nor will they suffer from headaches or eye ailments. One who gazes upon them continuously will never go blind. <image-desc>four red dots in a rhombus pattern</image-desc>.

There is a hollow star behind those bright stars named **The Handful of Grass** <note>original: *Adgha‘*</note>. It is fortunate, yielding light and full of joy. It has a cycle of thirty years, becoming great in a sign where there is reform, joy, great happiness, and blessing. Its dominion, however, brings the destruction of the "seven beasts"; when it descends into the sign of Leo, it destroys the predatory lions in that region during that year. Its image is <image-desc>three red dots in a triangle</image-desc>.

There is a black star known as **The Scraper** <note>original: *al-Sahi*</note>, solitary and without any accompanying stars. It is a star of astonishment, constant and non-returning. Its power is over the "affectionate ones" and the senses, the hearts, and the creatures of land, sea, and air. It does not affect humans. When it enters a water sign—such as Cancer, Pisces, or Scorpio—it is at its most powerful. Its image is <image-desc>single red dot</image-desc>.

As for Hermes—who encompassed everything with knowledge—he states that it does not wander into any land except those we have mentioned. It appears at seventeen degrees of Cancer <note>original: *al-Saratan*</note>. There are eight stars, and beside them is a great encircling star. They are hidden, resembling plants of the shore, appearing once every seven years. Their image is <image-desc>ten red dots arranged in a two-by-five grid</image-desc>.

There is a leaden-colored star whose hue is perpetually rough. It thunders and never rests because of its proximity to the Earth and the abundance of air surrounding it; thus, it is a "thunderer." Beside it are two luminous stars... named **The Herald** <note>original: *Khabar*</note> and **The Preeminent** <note>original: *al-Bari‘*</note>. They never descend into a sign opposite Aquarius <note>original: *al-Dalw*</note> without that year being one of thirst, hardship, and drought. Their image is <image-desc>three red dots in a triangle</image-desc>.

There is a star at the rear of Cancer that is red, luminous, and extremely bright. It approaches the "Hand of the Lucky One" and is called **The Harmonious One** <note>original: *Dhu al-I’tilaf*</note>. Hermes said that the one who turned the sphere made it a true light for any man who watches for it every night; he will not fail in his life until he dies, nor will his joy diminish. It has signs and indications: when it enters the sign of Virgo <note>original: *al-Sunbula*</note>, its owner prospers and rises high for a long time in every station. Its wrath is great, and it is a "distant" star, crossing the sphere in its own specific orbit once every three hundred years. Its image is <image-desc>single red dot</image-desc>.

There is a radiant star named **The Encompasser** <note>original: *Yuhut*</note>, drawn among the stars of the tribe of Hud. It has a radiance that, when it shines, almost veils everything around it. Its path follows the path of Sirius <note>original: *al-Shi‘ra*</note>.

<meta>This page continues the catalog of celestial bodies and their astrological portents. It moves from the descriptions of "The Fearful Ones" on the previous page to stars and comets associated with destruction, justice, and specific historical cycles of plagues and wars, frequently citing the authorities of Hermes Trismegistus and Ptolemy.</meta>

Its reign occurs every hundred years. When it enters the sign of **Scorpio** <note>original: *al-’Aqrab*</note>, it brings in that year pitch, blood, wars, and high prices for surveyors, as well as ruin, destruction, famine, hunger, and disease <note>original: *al-saqf*; likely a scribal error for *al-saqam*, meaning sickness</note>. Hermes named it **The Annihilator** <note>original: *al-Hanak*</note>. It is the star that rises before the "Outsider" <note>original: *al-Khariji*; potentially a reference to a specific planetary position or a political rebel</note>. Upon its rising, one hundred thousand people are killed by drowning, hunger, and thirst. Its image is <image-desc>cluster of seven red dots arranged in a circle</image-desc>.

There is another star called **The Thread** <note>original: *Mukhat*</note>. These are two stars with three small stars between them that are secretly sustained; they possess radiance and a fortunate light. When they alight in the sign of **Pisces** <note>original: *al-Hut*</note>, that year sees an abundance of water and fish—specifically fresh-sea fish—and their catch increases. Crops grow, and the Law <note>original: *al-Namus*; referring to divine or natural law</note> flourishes. The Nazarenes <note>original: *al-Nawwasir*; likely referring to the Nasoraeans or a specific astrological sect</note> named them. They traverse the sphere every eight full years and three and a half years. Their image is <image-desc>cluster of five red dots in a diamond/cross shape</image-desc>.

There is a yellow-colored star beneath the path of the Moon. When it is in the nearby mansions <note>term: Lunar Mansions (Manazil); the 28 divisions of the sky the moon passes through</note>, its "keys" are restrained. It is called **The Calamity** <note>original: *al-Dahiya*</note>. It completes a cycle every year. If it descends into one of the Zodiac signs, it breeds grief and misfortune for its owner, and a confrontation with a tyrannical, oppressive ruler. Ptolemy named it **The Hoopoe of Saghand** <note>original: *Hudhud Saghand*</note>. Its image is <image-desc>single red dot</image-desc>.

There are six bright stars toward the South, beautifully arranged. They have a cycle every ten years and descend into a sign. When they especially join **Jupiter** <note>original: *al-Mushtari*</note>, that year is one of justice, fairness, and joy for all people. They see every kind of goodness in their affairs and legal judgments. They have well-known signs for good fortune. Hermes named them **The Triangles** <note>original: *al-Muthallathat*</note>. Their image is <image-desc>cluster of four red dots</image-desc>.

There are two stars in the "Path of Corruption" among the "Followers." They are hidden; the eye can barely encompass or perceive them. Although hidden, they appear as two fortunate stars. They possess a "thread" <note>original: *mukhat*</note> in the sign of **Taurus** <note>original: *al-Thawr*</note>, and their owner remains in good fortune for four years. Hermes named them **The Two Greater Ones** <note>original: *al-Akbarayn*</note>. Their image is <image-desc>two red dots arranged vertically</image-desc>. They traverse the sphere every twelve years.

There is a star in the path of **Saturn** <note>original: *Zuhal*</note> in the heights. At its zenith is a star with a sweet glow known as **The Shimmer** <note>original: *al-Barham*</note>. Hermes named it **The Patient One** <note>original: *al-Sabir*</note> and also **The Watcher** <note>original: *al-Rahi*</note>. It has a light when it rises and a great radiance on the horizon. It has a "lock of hair" <note>a tail or mane common in comet descriptions</note> behind it, a cubit in length. It rises every hundred years, a blessed fortune that brings relief and joy. Its image is <image-desc>drawing of a golden-tipped spear or comet with a horizontal shaft</image-desc>.

There is another star known as **The Tailed Star** <note>original: *Kawkab al-Dhanab*; a common term for a comet</note>. It rises in the East every one hundred and seven years. It generates plagues and illnesses. It is red, leaning toward black. When it sets, the tails of things wither and fade. Its image is <image-desc>drawing of a comet with a dark red circular head and trailing multi-colored tail streaks</image-desc>.

There is another star known as **The Envoy** <note>original: *al-Waffad*</note>. Its cycle and span are long. The star itself cannot be seen because of the greatness of its branching; rather, one sees a light stretching across the sphere. It appears in the winter season during thunder and lightning, like large clouds. Its appearance occurs at the start of every fifty years. Its image is <image-desc>drawing of a symmetrical pointed tool or celestial symbol with a central bulb</image-desc>.

There are two unlucky stars known as **The Hooked Ones** <note>original: *al-Mu’taqafayn*</note>. They have a fine tail that casts flames and meteors. They are known as the **Gods of Clarity** <note>original: *ilah al-jala*</note>. When they rise, they remain for seven days, then return for another week. Their rising occurs at the start of every forty years. They indicate wood-gathering <note>possibly meaning drought/fuel shortage</note>, major events, and hypocrisy, especially if they are in places of evil or in the sign of **Pisces**. Their image is <image-desc>drawing of two parallel dark pointed needles or celestial symbols</image-desc>.

There is a star Hermes named **The Spear** <note>original: *al-Rumh*</note>. it also has three "clenched" tails. When it rises, it causes weakness in kings, especially the king of the land where it appears on the horizon. It indicates wars, plagues, loss of the "two powers," high prices, water scarcity, crop failure, and epilepsy—especially if it rises in the sign of **Aries** <note>original: *al-Hamal*; text is slightly unclear, possibly *al-Maraj*</note>, or in opposition or quadrature to it. If **Venus** <note>original: *al-Zahra*</note> is "laughing" <note>an astrological term for a favorable or bright position</note>, it indicates that the people and their plagues are stricken, and they fall into corruption and commit abominable acts if she is in her fall <note>term: Fall; the sign where a planet's influence is weakest</note> and **Mars** <note>original: *al-Marikh*</note> is in affliction.

<summary>This page catalogs various stars and comets, detailing their cyclical appearances (ranging from 1 to 107 years) and their astrological effects on society, including wars, plagues, and periods of justice. Matters were the opposite of the goodness and calm we previously mentioned, and this is its image: ۞

<image-desc>A celestial body, likely a comet, drawn in dark brown ink with a multi-pronged reddish tail extending horizontally to the left.</image-desc>

Regarding the collection of what Hermes mentioned of the "long-haired stars" <note>term: comets; historically referred to as stars with "locks" or "manes" of hair</note> and the *barūs* <note>original: *al-barūṣ*; likely a transliteration of a Greek or Persian astronomical term for a specific type of atmospheric or celestial phenomenon</note>. <unclear>It is said</unclear> that they spoil deeds from whence it is not felt and rectify them from whence it is not known. There are twenty-eight such stars. <unclear>It is said</unclear> that the one labeled *Ṭā’* <note>original: *ṭā’*; possibly an abbreviation or a specific classification in the author's source</note> has a tail. If it is positioned ahead of the Sun, its tail is in front of it; if it is behind the Sun, its tail follows behind. When it appears in the West, it extends and leaps toward the East, for it appears in whatever form the Sun dictates. If any of these appear in the "Burnt Path" <note>term: Via Combusta; a specific section of the zodiac (traditionally late Libra to early Scorpio) considered unlucky or "combust" in astrology</note>, its movement increases, becoming greater, faster in its passing, and closer to its eventual fading. If it is on the edge of the Galaxy—by "Galaxy" I mean the path of the Sun, the region of the celestial sphere called the Zodiac—I see it as a "pure house." It has a stationary point in its appearance where it turns and descends. If it is in the South, it is not seen clearly and hardly ever recedes. ۞

->Chapter Eight: On the Conditions of the Superior Planets<-

۞ Their distances, their lots, the dimensions of their bodies, and the descriptions of their forms along with their states.

Among these is **Saturn** <note>original: *Zuḥal*</note>. Its name in Persian is **Keyvān**, in Indian **Bishan-shar** <note>original: *Bishan-shar*; a corruption of *Shanishchara*, the Sanskrit name for Saturn</note>, in Greek **Phainon** <note>original: *Bāṭmānūs*; likely a scribal corruption of the Greek *Phainon*</note>, and in Latin **Kronos** <note>original: *Afrūnis*; likely a corruption of *Kronos*</note>. In the Arabic tongue, it is a masculine, malefic <note>term: malefic; an astrological term for a planet thought to bring difficulty or challenge</note>, "diurnal pair" star that emerges in the twelfth house. Its light extends nine degrees behind it and ahead of it. Its sphere is the First Sphere. Its great cycle is sixty-eight years <note>The text notes "sixty-eight," though traditional astrology often cites fifty-seven</note>, its middle cycle is fifty-seven years, and its small cycle is eighteen and a half years. Its exaltation is in **Libra** at twenty-one degrees, and its fall is in **Aries** at twenty-one degrees. 

In the human constitution, it governs the right ear, the spleen, the kidneys, hair, teeth, intestines, and bones. Among character traits, it governs vanity. Regarding tastes and foods, it governs everything with a foul or astringent taste, such as myrobalan <note>original: *al-halīlaj*; a fruit used in medieval medicine</note> and its likes. Among materials, it governs iron; among colors, pure black; in medicine, everything putrid; and among tastes, saltiness. In clothing, it governs black and red. Among wild beasts, it governs the fennec fox, monkeys, bears, pigs, locusts, and black cats. Among birds, it governs crows, eagles, cranes, and every long-necked bird, as well as the taste of the skunk <note>original: *al-ẓarbān*</note>. It governs cold, snow, ice, and dryness. Of the earth, it governs sharp and hard ground, fortresses, and among trees, every bitter and foul-smelling one. It governs black soil and buildings. Among professions, it governs those who cultivate the land and estates, the digging of wells and rivers, and the collection of land taxes. Among people, it governs women and elderly men. Of the climates, it governs the climate of India, the land of the Blacks <note>original: *al-Sūdān*</note>, the upper Hejaz, Babylon, the land of Persia, Khorasan, and Iraq. 

When it is the ruler of the year, it signifies the growth of plants, scorpions, foxes, rabbits, and moles. For this reason, it has been entrusted with the beauty that appears by night. Its nature is dominated by cold and dryness. Beyond the break of the Sun's circle, we account for the moisture of the Earth's vapors. It resides in the sphere of the fixed stars, and due to the speed of the stars' movement in its path from West to East, its revolution of the sphere occurs every two years <unclear>less some time</unclear>. The distance of its sphere from the Earth is eighteen million, ninety thousand, and two hundred and ten leagues <note>term: league (farsakh); a medieval unit of distance, roughly 3 miles or 5 kilometers</note>. It follows Jupiter in size. Its circumference measures seventeen thousand, eight hundred and ninety-two leagues. Its size is seventy-one times that of the Earth. Its detriment is in the sign of **Cancer**. It is the enemy of the luminous Sun and Mars, and the enemy of Venus.

# **Jupiter**
Its name in Persian is *Hormuz* <note>original: "Hurmuz"; the Middle Persian name for Jupiter, also associated with the deity Ahura Mazda</note>, in Indian it is *al-Qaris*, in Roman it is *Yuzawush* <note>original: "Yūzāwush"; a phonetic rendering of the Greek "Zeus"</note>, and in Roman also *Batrutr* <note>original: "Bāṭrūtr"; likely a corruption of a Greek or Latin epithet, possibly "Pater"</note>. It is a fortunate, masculine, diurnal planet <note>term: diurnal; in astrology, planets are categorized as "diurnal" (belonging to the day) or "nocturnal" (belonging to the night)</note>. Its lot is in the eleventh house. Its light—both behind and in front of it—extends nine degrees. It resides in the second sphere. Its period is fourteen years; its "Great Years" are seventy-nine, its "Middle Years" are forty-five, and its "Small Years" are twelve. Its exaltation <note>term: exaltation; the zodiac sign where a planet's influence is most harmoniously amplified</note> is in Cancer at fifteen degrees, and its fall <note>term: fall; the sign opposite the exaltation where the planet's power is weakened</note> is in Capricorn at fifteen degrees.

Among moral traits, it governs pride. From the human body, it rules the stomach, the left ear, the spine, the heart, and the veins. Among animals, it rules sheep. Among plants, everything sweet. Among stones, the pearl. Among scents, frankincense and cassia <note>original: "al-salīkha"; a fragrant bark similar to cinnamon</note>. Among tastes, the fatty or rich. Among beasts, horses, female camels, and Bactrian camels. It is associated with white and yellow clothing. Its character is magnificent and airy. It governs cities, ministers, judges, jurists, people of knowledge and goodness, fatherhood, and swords. Among lands, it rules the plains and winds; among trees, every fruit-bearing tree. Its taste is that of fresh water. All planets are in friendship with it except for Mars. It pushes with its back toward Saturn. 

Its latitude in diurnal births—if the zodiac sign is masculine—and its years in the second state are seventy-seven years. It possesses forbearance, knowledge, piety, and intellect. It governs religious jewelry <unclear>adorned</unclear> with pearls. Among birds, it rules peacocks, roosters, francolins, chickens, pigeons, ducks, and ostriches. It grants its fortune to those of "redness" in their livelihood and others of "whiteness" in their livelihood. Its nature is predominantly moderate and well-balanced. Its sphere is situated between the spheres of Saturn and Mars—between the burning nature and the cooling nature—and its specific power within itself is warmth. For this reason, it is the cause for the growth of beneficial, dispersing winds. 

Its movement is from West to East, and it completes one revolution of the sphere every twelve years. The distance of its sphere from the Earth is 110,541,400 <note>The text likely refers to miles or farsakhs in medieval units</note>. It is one of the most awe-inspiring of the "second category" of planets in size. The circumference of its body is 16,708 *farsakhs* <note>term: farsakh; an ancient unit of distance, roughly 3 to 4 miles</note>, and it is seventy-five times the size of the Earth. It indicates righteousness, worship, austerity, monasticism, asceticism, generosity, noble crafts, beauty, guidance, purity, virtuous deeds, ministers, the justice of the great, ambition, mercy, piety, praise, a good reputation, and an abundance of children, wealth, precious gems, knowledge, forbearance, and sound judgment. It is at its most fortunate when it is in the sign of Pisces. Soft clothing is suitable to it.

# **Mars**
Its name in Persian is *Bahram*, in Roman it is *Artes* <note>original: "Artes"; a phonetic rendering of the Greek "Ares"</note>, in Indian it is *al-Baghi*, and in Greek it is *Mahatos* <note>original: "Mahāṭos"; likely a phonetic rendering of "Pyroeis," the Greek name for Mars</note>. It is an unfortunate, fiery, nocturnal, "pair" planet <note>original: "zawj laylī"</note>. It emerges in the sixth house. Its light, behind and in front, is eight degrees. It resides in the third sphere. Its period is four years. Its "Great Years" are one hundred and eight, its "Middle Years" are forty and a half, and its "Small Years" are fifteen. Its exaltation is in Capricorn at twenty-eight degrees, and its fall is in Cancer at twenty-eight degrees. Its detriment <note>term: detriment; the zodiac sign opposite the one a planet naturally rules, where it is considered "out of place"</note> is Libra and Taurus.

It governs actions of seizure and violence. From the human body, it rules the liver, blood, grief, the eyes, the *naqira* <note>original: "al-nāqira"; the hollow at the base of the skull or the back of the neck</note>, and the flesh. Among colors, it rules red. Among tastes, the bitter and the sour. Among stones, copper. Among animals, those that are black or yellow. Among scents, it is suited by anything acrid and foul-smelling. Among wild beasts, it rules leopards, panthers, eagles, wasps, spiders, and scorpions. Among birds, every wild bird or those close to predatory strength. 

Its nature is of heat, dryness, and fire. Among social states, it governs commanders, soldiers, border guards, the army, theft, pursuit, and the shedding of blood. Among lands, it rules the rugged and difficult terrain. Among trees, those that are thorny. It pushes with its back toward Jupiter.

...and the trial of the age and the trial of certain planets. When the year begins under the influence of **Mars**, dogs and wolves increase in number; dens are stirred, as are panthers and predatory beasts that eat meat. The nature of Mars is dryness and heat, with its dryness being the dominant quality derived from the Sun. It possesses a specific sphere and boundary in the heavens, which is its moving sphere. 

Its path travels from West to East, completing one full revolution every year and a half. Its speed over a single year is 7,420,890 parasangs <note>term: parasang (farsakh); an ancient unit of distance, roughly 3.5 miles or 5.5 kilometers</note> during its greatest journey. The distance it covers in a single day is 4,862 parasangs, and it circles the Earth once and a half [in its period]. It signifies wicked deeds such as the formation of gangs, blacksmithing, industry, and printers. It also relates to the news of the khan <note>term: khan; a roadside inn for travelers and merchants</note>, falconry, wandering vagabonds, barbers, and the severing of limbs <note>original: "al-a'sab"; likely referring to nerves or tendons, here in the context of injury</note>, highway robbery, and the corruption of things. It has a malefic influence on the fire signs because they share its nature. Its power is greatest in Leo, yet it is also most harmful to the sign of Leo due to the intensity of its violence. Venus is somewhat related to Mars as she rules over him. Mars is an enemy in the West because it is a fiery planet that signifies amputation, distress, orphanhood, sickness, injustice, and oppression.

**The Sun**
Its name in Persian is *Mehr* <note>original: "Mehr"; the Sun deity in ancient Iranian culture</note>, in Roman [Greek] it is *Helios*, and in Indian it is *al-Sharif* <note>meaning: "The Noble One"</note>. It is a fortunate, masculine, diurnal planet. Its joy is in the ninth house. Its light shines from behind. Its station is fifteen degrees. It is swift in Aries at nineteen degrees [its exaltation], its fall is in Libra at nineteen degrees, and its detriment <note>term: detriment (wabal); the zodiac sign opposite the one a planet rules, where it is most "uncomfortable" or weak</note> is in Aquarius.

From the human body, it governs the nerves, flesh, fat, veins, semen, brain, and the hair of the head. Among colors, it rules yellow; among tastes, the pungent. Among metals, it rules gold. It is the guardian of the eyes. Among perfumes, it rules musk; among foods, honey. Among clothing, it suits fine yellow garments. Among beasts, it rules lions, wolves, hyenas, and deer. It was created from the Great Light. Among minerals, it rules gold. Among trees, it rules the large ones with many branches. It avoids all planets except Mars, for it neither confirms nor leads it <note>interpretive note: this likely refers to specific astrological aspects or "combustion" where planets near the sun lose their power</note>, but its management is handed over to Mars and Jupiter. For this reason, it is found in the diurnal births within masculine signs. 

One can deduce from it power, victory, authority, and subjugation. Its journey each night is one degree and a quarter. Within it reside goodness and beauty. Among stations, it rules kings, nobles, great men, and princes. It possesses mines of gold and jewels. Among birds, it rules eagles, hawks, and falcons. It grants life; in its assistance, it grants a full lifespan. If it is looked upon by a fortunate planet, it grants the newborn a perfect life of one hundred and twenty years. Among jewelry, it rules that which adorns the head. The nature of the Sun is heat and dominant dryness. Its sphere is the middle one among the seven spheres. Mars, in its time, is below it. Its station moves one degree every day. The Highest Sphere <note>term: the Primeum Mobile; the outermost sphere that moves all others</note> rotates it from East to West. The circumference of the Sun's sphere is 758,770,000 parasangs <note>Note: The transcription "ثلثا ربع ألف ألف" is mathematically complex; the translator has rendered the intended large scale</note>. The circumference of its physical body is 10,960 parasangs. In size, it is fifteen times [the Earth].

**Venus**
Its name in Greek is *Kafatos* <note>likely a corruption of "Kypris," a common Greek epithet for Aphrodite/Venus</note>, in Persian *Bende Bakht*, in Indian *Aba Hind*, and in Roman *Malud Baka*. It is a fortunate, feminine planet of love. It ascends in the assembly and its light is behind it. Its station is seven degrees. It resides in the fifth sphere. Its cycle is 800 days. Its "Great Years" are eighty-two, its "Middle Years" are forty-five, and its "Small Years" are eight. Its exaltation is in Pisces at twenty-seven degrees, and its fall is in Virgo at twenty-seven degrees. Its detriment is in Scorpio and Aries.

From the human body, it rules the bladder, semen, the hand, desire, pleasure, and scents. Among actions, it rules joy, elegance, singing, and perfume. Among colors, it rules joyful ones. Among tastes, the fatty and the bitter. It rules mines of brass. Among plants, it rules roses and basil. Among sweet foods, it rules [various treats]. Among beasts, it rules gazelles, buffalo, and [unclear]. Among birds, it rules ducks, falcons, swallows, bats, and owls. Among creeping things, it rules beetles and their likeness. Its light is white. It possesses heat and moisture. The other planets love it...

...Saturn <note>original: "al-arhal"; likely a scribal error for "Zuhal," the Arabic name for Saturn</note>, for he is her enemy. She <note>Venus</note> hands over her management to the Sun, in whom guidance is hoped for. She is masculine <note>interpretive note: while Venus is generally feminine, in certain astrological traditions or house placements, its gendered influence can shift</note> and rejoices in nocturnal births when she is in a feminine sign. Her age of full strength is fifty-two years. Her qualities include delicacy, kindness, beauty, luxury, and affection. She governs the jewelry suitable for women. When the year begins under her influence, weak livestock multiply, as do all creatures in the water, including fish, aquatic beasts, and birds. Her colors are green and white. She is of a balanced temperament and moderate power, though she is the mistress of the waters. Out of affection for her proximity to the Sun, she is built [as she is]. She completes one revolution every ten months. The distance of her sphere from the Earth is 1,029,705 parasangs <note>term: parasang (farsakh); an ancient unit of distance, roughly 3.5 miles</note>. In terms of size and mass, the diameter of her location is 499 parasangs, which is seventy-five and a fraction units of the Earth's size.

**Mercury**
Its name in Persian is *Tir*, in Indian *Al-Wasir* <note>original: "al-wasir"; likely referring to the Sanskrit "Budha"</note>, and in Roman *Aristo* <note>original: "Aristo"; likely referring to Aristotle, as Mercury was the patron of logic and wisdom, or a corruption of Hermes</note>. It is a planet of mixed nature: male with males, female with females, malefic with malefics, and benefic with benefics. Its joy is in the Ascendant. Its light extends seven degrees behind and before it. It resides in the sixth sphere. Its period of revolution is two years <note>original: "ithnatay 'ashar sanatayn"; unclear, but likely referring to its cycle through the zodiac</note>. Its Great Year is seventy-six, its Middle Year is one hundred and four <note>unclear: text says "one hundred and four and six"</note>, and its Small Year is twenty years. Its exaltation <note>term: exaltation (sirr/sharaf); the zodiac sign where a planet's influence is most powerful</note> is in Virgo at fifteen degrees, and its fall <note>term: fall (hubut); the sign where a planet is weakest</note> is in Pisces at fifteen degrees. Its detriment <note>term: detriment (wabal); a sign of unfavorable influence</note> is in Pisces and Sagittarius. It governs trickery, deception, and the following parts of the body: the spleen, the right nostril, the gall bladder, the arteries, and the nerves. 

It loves the light of the heavens. Its associated mineral is quicksilver <note>original: "al-zibaq"; mercury</note>. It opposes the group in its light. In medicine, it governs the skilled and the physicians. Among wild animals, it governs the weak ones, the panthers, and the *barabir* among the cattle, as well as mountain sheep, crocodiles, and turtles. Among birds, it governs sparrows, hoopoes, amber-birds, collared doves, wood pigeons, and every bird with dry, black flesh that beats its wings. Among swarming creatures, it governs moths, flies, and crickets. It has a voice distinct from other natures. 

It hands over its management to all planets except the Moon. It appears with the Sun in the East and with the Moon in the West. Its years are the years of the father-in-law <note>original: "al-hamah"; possibly a typo for a technical term</note>, seventy-six years. It is the master of etiquette, calculation, writing, commerce, time, intelligence, speech, fine crafts, and missions; it acts as a messenger in wondrous works. Among foods, it governs the *farifid* dish <note>unclear: a specific historical recipe</note>. Among jewelry, it governs that which is made of ivory for women. It is at rest due to its longitudinal proximity to the Sun, and its essence is moistened due to the proximity of its sphere to that of the Moon. It traverses each zodiac sign in twenty-seven days. It rejoices with the Sun in the East and with the Moon in the West. It governs magic and Sindh, and among lands, it governs ashen soil. When the year begins under its influence, physicians increase, and the fever of wild beasts, donkeys, and the womb becomes frequent. It completes a revolution in the sphere every seven months. The distance of its sphere from the Earth is 950,000 parasangs. It follows Venus in size; the measurement of its diameter is 118 parasangs, which is 510 units of the Earth. 

It signifies the intellect, hidden depth, the moderation of affairs, the derivation of sciences, books, eloquence, ease, oratory, and the philosophical crafts such as medicine, astronomy, geometry, land-surveying, and surgical texts. Among the delicate crafts, it governs engraving and embroidery. It is the opposite of Mars, being his contrary in vision, location, creation, etiquette, and intellect. Mercury agrees with Venus in the heart when it is descending in its sphere. It is one of the planets that bring rain.

**The Moon**
Its name in Persian is *Mah*, in Roman *Taknish*, in Indian *Saih*, and in Greek *Motos*. It is a benefic, solitary, nocturnal planet. It rejoices in the third house. Its light extends twelve degrees behind and before it. Its sphere is the seventh. Its *Fardariyah* <note>term: Fardariyah; a Persian-Hellenistic system of planetary periods ruling over blocks of years</note> is nine years. Its Great Year is one hundred and eight, its Middle Year is nine, and its Small Year is twenty-five years. Its exaltation is in Taurus at three degrees, and its fall is in Scorpio at three degrees. Its detriment is in Capricorn <note>original: "al-hara"; likely a corruption of "al-Jadi"</note>. Among states of being, it governs speech. In the human body, it governs the lungs, the left eye, the brain, and the mouth...

...and the skin, and a love for beautiful lights <note>original: "an-anwar al-nadhira"; referring to radiant or flourishing light</note> when it is full of its light, and brown <note>original: "al-asmar"; dusky or dark-complexioned</note> when it is waning. Its taste is saltiness. Among metals, it governs silver. 

In terms of distribution, its properties are shared with gold. In medicine, it governs everything pleasant or fragrant. Regarding food, it governs butter and ghee. Among wild beasts and cattle, it governs the gentle ones. In terms of crafts, it is diligent in its early stages <note>original: "bikuratihi"</note> and is associated with beekeeping, grocers, and dogs. Among mounts, it governs gray work-horses and sables. As for birds, it governs mountain birds; it does not govern water birds, though it oversees cats and swallows. Its suitable clothing includes red and white. Its emanation is moist light. Its nature is both hot and cold, and it is the keeper of news, coldness, play, and correspondence. It governs waters, ponds, and rivers. Among plants, it governs grass and anything without a stalk. It is the companion of Jupiter and is hostile to all other planets. It repels the ill-fortune of every planet and sparks good fortune in the horoscopes of the blessed when its affection is clear. 

For the seeker, it is submissive and does not perceive its own limit <note>interpretive note: this likely refers to the Moon reflecting light rather than generating its own</note>. In its journey, it is dominant, crossing each zodiac sign in twenty-five and a half hours. Its day is considered feminine <note>historical note: Monday is traditionally the day of the Moon in astrology</note>. Its terrestrial domain is the "sixth division," including the lands of the Maghreb, Africa, and Al-Andalus <note>historical note: medieval geographers often mapped planets to specific world regions</note>. It is one of the "planets of rain." Its nature is the wind, and its major cycle is one hundred and eight years. In medicine, it is associated with grapes. Its gemstone is the ruby <note>original: "yaqut"</note>. It is a benefic by aspect, warm in conjunction, and its earthly nature is moist and soft. 

Its sphere <note>the celestial path it travels</note> is a cycle around the Earth. It governs the seas and oversees the needs of physical bodies and their necks. Its sphere is elevated above the sphere of Mercury; it completes its celestial rotation in twenty-eight days. Its sphere is the closest of all the heavens to the Earth. The extent of the Moon’s shadow-sphere is 122,612 parasangs <note>term: parasang (farsakh); an ancient unit of distance, roughly 3.5 miles</note>. The Moon is a stone of fortune; its mass is a fraction of the Earth's. It resides in the axis, descending from one part to another.

## Chapter Nine: Regarding the Lunar Mansions and Their States
### Their specific actions, along with an explanation of their weather-signs, dry winds, forms, and consequences.

<note>term: anwa' (weather-signs); an ancient pre-Islamic system of predicting weather and seasons based on the rising and setting of specific stars</note>

The first of these is **Al-Sharatayn** <note>original: "the two signs"; marking the beginning of the zodiac</note>, also called *Al-Nath* <note>meaning "the butting," as these stars represent the horns of Aries the Ram</note>. It consists of three stars in the shape of an inverted triangle, like the teeth of a green [comb]. Two are the "fish"... and the last [location of] the Moon passed in the third magnitude <note>term: magnitude; the brightness of a star on a scale of 1 to 6</note>, while the one with it is in the fifth magnitude, and the other is a northern, red star in the third magnitude. The "bonds" of this mansion include "the Body" <note>original: "al-badan"</note>, which is a star the size of the Pleiades. There rises with it from the direction of the Levant a star called *Al-Khassaf* <note>the Dispeller</note>, a large star among the triangular stars, and the "Elbow" of the Pleiades. For the Moon, there passed from the southern side the "First Frog" <note>note: often associated with the star Fomalhaut or Beta Ceti</note>, also called "the Dog," which is of the first magnitude on the foot of the "Stoned Aquarius." There are stars called "the Cows," five in number, and with them a passing star called *Al-Udhrah* <note>the Maidenhead</note>, which precedes *Al-Sharatayn* in rising and is part of Capricorn.

<margin>The Inlet <note>original: "al-khawr"</note></margin>
<margin>This is the twenty-third day of the "Signs" <note>al-shurut</note>, which is the eighth of April <note>Nisan</note>.</margin>

<image-desc>
A diagram of stars and constellations related to the lunar mansions. Red dots represent the stars, with labels in Arabic script.
- On the far left: a single dot labeled "The Ghoul's Head" <note>original: "Ras al-Ghul" / Algol</note>.
- To the right: a triangular arrangement of three dots labeled "The Dispeller" <note>original: "Al-Khassaf"</note> and "The Butting of the Ram" <note>original: "Nattah al-Hamal"</note>.
- Further right: a single dot labeled "The Elbow" <note>original: "Al-Mirfaq"</note>.
- Central right: two dots labeled "The Two Signs" <note>original: "Al-Sharatayn"</note>.
- Far right: a cluster of dots (three large, three small) labeled "The Little Belly" <note>original: "Al-Butayn"</note> and "The Palm of the Hand" <note>original: "Kaffa Kadriya"</note> below.
- Far right edge: a label "The Scorpion" <note>original: "Al-Aqr"</note>.
Arced red and black lines connect or delineate the regions between these star groups.
</image-desc>

**Al-Butayn** <note>meaning "the little belly" of the Ram</note>. Then *Al-Butayn* rises; it consists of three stars like a triangle, though one of them is of the fourth magnitude and two are trembling <note>unsteady or flickering light</note>, rising from the west on its left. The bonds of this mansion from the northern side are the "Shoulder of the Pleiades," which are parallel. A star rises called *Al-Rajaz*, and beside it are small stars. The "Pillar of the Earth" rises, also called "The Book," a passing star. Then "The Stage" <note>original: "al-marhalah"</note> rises, which consists of four small square stars, and from the south, it crosses the Frog.

<vocab>Lunar Mansions (al-manazil al-qamariyya), Al-Sharatayn (the two signs), Al-Butayn (the little belly), Al-Nath (the butting), Al-Thurayya (Pleiades), Sphere of the Moon (falk al-qamar), Mercury (Utarid), Nisan (April), Ras al-Ghul (Algol), Al-Mirfaq (the elbow), Parasang (farsakh)</vocab>

<summary>This page concludes the astrological and physical profile of the Moon—detailing its governance over silver, the Maghreb, and the tides—before introducing the first two Lunar Mansions, Al-Sharatayn and Al-Butayn. It provides specific astronomical magnitudes and distances, as well as the traditional weather-sign (anwa') associations for these star clusters.</summary>
<keywords>Moon, astrology, Lunar Mansions, Al-Sharatayn, Al-Butayn, medieval astronomy, parasang, star magnitude, weather-signs, geography</keywords>
