<meta>This page transitions from a list of geographical locations to a thematic discussion on the nature and medicinal properties of water, followed by a specific survey of major rivers in the Near East and Caucasus regions.</meta>

# Chapter Nineteen: On the Description of Rivers

There is no need to describe the shape of every river in every region of the earth, for that would take us too long and the book would stray from its intended brevity and the constraints of the knowledge required. We shall begin with its general nature, for water is, in our estimation, a precious jewel because of how it is sourced, its paths, and its aid. It acts upon the winds like the restoration of the soul; it is the strength and the body of everything that grows, whether animal or plant, being of the same genus and equal to them. A unique quality of water is that every drink, no matter how refined, clear, or sweet, returns to its watery origin. Were it not for water, animals would perish, as their existence depends on it for life, strength, pasture, and all that befalls them. To the observer, the sight of running water brings clarity to the eye and rest to the heart. When it is full, it reveals wonders—bringing forth pearls and gems—and when it is pure and pleasant, it improves the constitution. It removes from the valleys any impurity or stagnant water. When the "drop" of life is created from water, it is called water. The sense of touch finds it gentle, and it is pleasant to the mouths of those who drink it. It is the basis for all processed goods like sugar and others, and it helps food pass quickly through the esophagus and aids the physical appearance of bodies and their washing. The Prophet (peace be upon him) said: "Water is not defiled by anything." It is the goodness of all things and one of the **four elements** <term>four elements: The classical elements of Earth, Water, Air, and Fire, believed to be the building blocks of all matter.</term>.

It is said that the best of waters is rainwater taken in a clean cloth. If it falls upon a mountain and gathers on a rock, it is like the water of great rivers or the water of a large, deep pool. Aside from this, moderate hot water is suitable for everything. As for the water found from hail in the summer, it should not be drunk. If rainwater is taken in a foreign vessel and drunk by someone suffering from the liver or jaundice <term>jaundice: original "al-yaraqan," a yellowing of the skin or eyes often linked to liver issues</term>, it benefits them. If it is taken in a bowl before it touches the ground and is drunk repeatedly, it benefits the preservation of knowledge. If it is mixed with salt, honey, and mastic, it removes white vitiligo <term>vitiligo: original "al-bahq," a condition causing loss of skin pigment</term>.

If water from hail is taken and placed on a "Persian reed" <note>likely *Arundo donax*, used in various medicinal preparations</note>, burned, and applied, it benefits those suffering from dental decay and strengthens the teeth. If hail water is taken with a person’s sweat and given to someone suffering from rumbling in the gut, it calms them. If it is given with the milk of a donkey and a goat, it calms <unclear>palpitations</unclear> of the heart. If dusty sulfur is taken and smeared with it, it removes the mange. If ash is taken, mixed with water, and smeared on black vitiligo, it removes it. If the water of frozen ice is taken and wheat or iron are soaked in it, then cooked and eaten, it benefits the "winds of the soul" <note>this likely refers to flatulence or internal gases</note> and treats pain of the heart and stomach. As for the water of the sulfuric springs, it benefits the groin and hemorrhoids if they are cleaned... <note>The text here becomes slightly fragmented with references to sponges, caper roots, and goat's bile in relation to eye pain.</note> Sulfuric water also benefits the mange. As for salt water, if taken with honey and cyperus <note>original: "al-su'd," an aromatic plant used in medicine</note> and rubbed on the tongue, it cuts away the heat and the phlegm. And God knows best.

-> **Discourse on the Rivers** <-

The **Kura** and the **Aras** <note>original: "al-Kurr wa al-Rass"; two major rivers in the Caucasus</note> both flow from the mountains of Azerbaijan, or from Musnad and Shahrizor. The Aras and the Kura are two great rivers whose source emerges from the mountains in a place called Daraj <note>literally "The Steps"</note>, also known as the Meeting of the Two Seas, and they pour into the Sea of Jurjan <term>Sea of Jurjan: The historical name for the Caspian Sea.</term> like the Sindh.

Then the **Arnud** river <note>Referring to the Zayanderud</note> joins the Kura river; it is the valley of Isfahan, emerging from it and irrigating its districts, which are seventeen districts. Then it returns to a desert at its beginning and emerges in Kerman at a distance of sixty leagues. The **Sayhan** <note>The Seyhan River in modern Turkey</note> is a river whose beginning is from the lands of the Byzantines <note>original: "al-Rum"</note> and its mouth is in the Levantine Sea <term>Levantine Sea: The eastern Mediterranean.</term>. The source of the **Jayhan** <note>The Ceyhan River</note>—which is the river of Mopsuestia <note>original: "al-Massisa"</note>—is also from the lands of the Byzantines and it pours into the "Sea of the Tongue." The **River of the Blacks** <note>likely referring to the Orontes, often associated with the region of the "Zanj" or "Blacks" in some geographical traditions, or a transcription error for "Nahr al-Asi"</note> pours into the Levantine Sea behind Antioch. This is the **Jordan**, and it is known as the "Inverted River" <note>The Orontes is famously called "al-Asi" (the Rebellious/Inverted) because it flows north unlike the other rivers in the region.</note>; its beginning is from the districts of Sawa, and it flows southwards to pour into the Roman Sea <term>Roman Sea: Another historical name for the Mediterranean.</term>.

<summary>This page discusses the spiritual and medicinal properties of water according to medieval Islamic tradition, followed by a geographical overview of major rivers like the Kura, Aras, Seyhan, and the Orontes.</summary>

# Chapter Twenty: On the Wonders of Mermaids, Fish, and Sea Monsters

**The Transformed One** <term>Arabic: *Masakh*; referring to a creature transformed into another shape</term> is found in the lands of Champa <note>original: *al-Sanf*, a historical kingdom in modern-day Vietnam</note>. It is a fish whose face resembles a human face, soft above the water, while beneath it is a fish with a head like a sandal.

**The Anqar** and the **Satar** and a fish also found in Champa, from which a body emerges called **The Master of the Impression.** <note>likely the Remora or 'Suckerfish'</note> Despite its small size, when this fish appears, it can halt a great ship with all its sails unfurled by attaching itself to the ship's prow. It will not allow it to move even if a multitude of people pull it or every wind strikes it, until the fish releases it. <margin>Al-Nath <note>The Butting; the first lunar mansion</note></margin> 

->**Conclusion of the Chapter on Animals**<-
->**Those that Resemble the Mansions of the Moon**<-

A beast called the **Mook** has the face of a human, with a tuft of hair on its head. It has the body of a bird with two wings and two legs, and from its middle to its end, it has the tail of a dog.

A beast called **Shin Rabita** has two tails, two human hands, two ears, and two wings. On its neck <note>OCR reads *laghwihi*, likely *'unuqihi* (neck)</note> is a human head with hair like a cap. It is named after **Al-Butayn** <note>The Little Belly; the second lunar mansion</note>.

A beast called **Farsa** is in the image of a woman sitting cross-legged. On her head is a small crown, her hair is loose, and her hand is placed toward her breast. It is named after **Al-Thurayya** <note>The Pleiades; the third lunar mansion</note>.

<margin>Al-Dabaran <note>The Follower; the fourth lunar mansion</note></margin> A beast called **Babris** is in the shape of a scorpion with a human face raised upon its back. It raises its legs and stays alone, reaching the ponds where it lives in its burrow; above its head is a feather.

<margin>Al-Haq’a <note>The White Spot; the fifth lunar mansion</note></margin> A beast called **Bitfarq** is in the shape of a crocodile. Half of its face from the right to the chest is human, sitting upright, and from its middle to its bottom, it is a fish of every name <note>perhaps meaning it has various fish-like features</note>.

<margin>Al-Han’a <note>The Brand; the sixth lunar mansion</note></margin> A beast called **Bus** is in the image of a woman with her left hand on her cheek, and above the hand, on her right palm, is her head. Her middle to her bottom is the body of a permanent fish.

A beast called **Kash** is in the image of a woman sitting cross-legged. On her head is a circular crown, and in her lap is a lute <note>original: *'oud*</note> which she plays. She has small loose hairs, and she is named after **Al-Dhira’** <note>The Arm; the seventh lunar mansion</note>.

<margin>Al-Nathra <note>The Sneeze/Nose-hair; the eighth lunar mansion</note></margin> A beast called **Jawsh** has half a face, and on its head is a pointed cap that leans to one side. From its middle to its end, it has the tail of a puppy, two legs, and its tail points down toward the moon.

A beast called **Lakhs** is in the image of a prominent dog's head with the body of a bird, having two wings and two legs. It has a scent from behind it <note>unclear reading</note> extending to the tip of the tail. It passes the second head... black and made in the name of **Al-Nathra**.

A beast called **Ghal** is in the image of a great head with hair and a beard, and the thighs of a predatory beast connected to its neck, with two legs and talons. It is named after **Al-Jabhah** <note>The Forehead; the tenth lunar mansion</note>.

A beast called **Nush** is the image of a human face with a dark complexion and the body of a bird. It is named after **Al-Kharatan** <note>The Two Ribs; the eleventh lunar mansion</note>.

A beast called **Samghaz** is in the image of a beast’s head with ears, a neck, and wings. Its middle and end are the body of a lion, but ending in the body of a thick, coiled snake. It is named after **Al-Sarfah** <note>The Change; the twelfth lunar mansion</note>.

A beast called **Habnas** has the head of a lion with an open mouth and visible fangs. Its body behind is that of a lion with wings, and in its throat <note>OCR: *laghwihi*</note> it has a human head with hair and a beard. It is named after **Al-’Awwa** <note>The Barker; the thirteenth lunar mansion</note>.

<margin>Simak al-Ramih <note>Arcturus; the 'Uplifted One of the Spearman'</note></margin> A beast called **Raks** has a head like two heads joined at the back with a long beard. In its right hand, it grips a human head, held by talons. It has stretched its other hand behind it. Its middle is a fish with a tail.

A beast called **Hams** is in the image of a human head with a beard and two ears... its body is the body of a bird with wings, two legs, and a tail. It is named after **Al-Ghafr** <note>The Covering; the fifteenth lunar mansion</note>.

A beast called **Auras** is in the image of a bird with a broken beak. It has two bodies <note>reading *tana* as *thina*</note> facing each other, and they have feathers like the cells of a hive <note>or "knots"</note>. They have wings behind their backs and their legs are bent. It is named after **Al-Zubana** <note>The Claws; the sixteenth lunar mansion</note>.

A beast called **Katrun** is in the image of a ram’s face with much hair on its head, resembling a cow. Down to its side, it has a rough body covered in skin. This is named after **Al-Iklil** <note>The Crown; the seventeenth lunar mansion</note>.

A beast called **Al-Tush** is in the image of a human face with a small beard. Its body to the middle is... and its body has a tail at the spine reaching down. Below the palm, it has fingers, and from its middle is a body without a head and a bird’s tail. It is named after **Al-Qalb** <note>The Heart; the eighteenth lunar mansion</note>.

A beast called **Radhl** is in the image of a human with two ears like those of a fox. Its body from the middle downwards is... and from its middle downwards— <margin>Al-Shawla <note>The Raised Tail; the nineteenth lunar mansion</note></margin> —it is the image of a thick snake.

A beast called **Kan** is in the image of a woman with wide braids. On her head is a circular crown, and she has the body of a bird with wings, resembling an ostrich <note>original: *na'am*</note>.

A beast called the **Humayran** is in the form of a human, except from the chest down to its liver it is shaped like a sword. Its left hand is extended with an open palm as if it were a left-hand support. <note>This creature represents the fourth lunar mansion, **Al-Dabaran** (The Follower)</note>. Its hindquarters have a sting resembling the mansion **Sa’d al-Dhabih** <note>The Lucky One of the Slaughterer; a star in Capricorn</note>.

A beast called the **Salawat** is in the image of a human head with protruding teeth. Its body is like that of a leopard, its hands are human hands, and its frame is crooked like a dam. <margin>They are descended from Sudanese maidservants</margin> <note>This creature represents the fifth lunar mansion, **Al-Haq’ah** (The Circle of Hair)</note>.

A beast called the **Qarqaf** is in the image of a half-human; it has the leg of a human and a tail. At the end of its head are many smaller heads resembling the fronds of a palm tree. <note>This creature represents the sixth lunar mansion, **Al-Han’ah** (The Brand)</note>.

A beast called the **Lahus** is in the image of a human whose face is turned toward the right. It holds the handle of a bucket with a wrapped cord that resembles a twisted rope. <note>This creature represents the seventh lunar mansion, **Al-Dhira’** (The Arm)</note>.

A beast called the **Sea-Horse** <note>original: *Faras Bahr*</note> is in the image of a human except for its face. In its hand is a skin-bag made of hides, and its snout is long, resembling a flute or a reed-pipe. <note>This creature represents the eighth lunar mansion, **Al-Nathrah** (The Sneeze)</note>.

A beast called the **Tafras** is in the image of a human with the body of a fish. It is covered in spikes and its belly resembles that of a whale. <note>This creature represents the ninth lunar mansion, **Al-Tarf** (The Glance)</note>.

# In the Persian Sea

There are turtles whose circumference is twenty cubits, or more or less. Sometimes the belly of a turtle is found to contain ten eggs, or more or less. They may grow so large that they are mistaken for an island. There is a fish called the **Haratim** <note>original: *al-Haratim*; the Sawfish</note> which has a beak like a long snout that can pierce ships; its beak has teeth like the teeth of a saw. In this sea is also a fish called the **Atum** <note>original: *al-Atum*; the Dugong or Sea-cow</note>. It has genitals like those of a woman and hair like human hair. It has no scales, and its face is like the face of a sow. There is also a scorpion that mimics the land scorpion but has two heads and a tail made of solid stone; if it bites a human, they die. There is a fish shaped like a cow whose hide is used to make shields; it is said to be pure ivory-like skin. There is a fish called the **Dakhs** <note>original: *al-Dakhs*; the Dolphin or Porpoise</note> which mimics the *arfa* <note>possibly a reference to a specific movement or another sea creature</note>. There is also a fish that, when the sea becomes turbulent, emerges from the depths and appears in the waves; the sailors recognize it by this, and it is called the **Farastuj** <note>original: *al-Farastuj*; likely the Flying Fish, named after the swallow bird</note>.

In the Indian Sea and the Persian Sea, there is a type of fish called the **Wal** <note>original: *al-Waal*; the Whale</note>. Its length is a hundred cubits, more or less. Ships and those aboard them often fear it, but they know how to handle it; it only intends to cross from one side of the ship to the other. To avoid being struck, the crew beats on brass pots, drums, and pails, clanging them together until the whale flees. When it dives, it draws in water as if it were a descending valley. When it closes its mouth, the water is strained through its teeth, and it gurgles like a water-skin, leaving the smaller fish inside its belly.

The whale has an enemy called the **Shakk** fish. It is small, about one or two cubits long. This fish stalks the whale until it is distracted, then enters its ear <note>original: *simakh*</note> and stings it. When the whale feels this, it dives to the very bottom of the sea until it dies, while the small fish remains attached to its place.

There are also those who hunt the whale. They tie a rope to its ear, which is like a thick cord. They wait for it in small boats and pile fragrant herbs upon it. When the hunter wishes to catch it, he releases it from its pen and holds the rope on a winch, then sends it toward a larger fish just as a falconer releases a hawk <note>original: *al-bashaq*</note>. It attaches itself to the whale’s ear. The whale dives into the sea and struggles, then surfaces and dives again. The hunter slackens the rope for it, then pulls it up. The fish continues to eat at the whale's ear, and the whale cannot stop it until it weakens and its senses fail. Then the hunter takes it by force, removes the small fish from its ear, and returns it to its pen. In this sea, there is also a fish that comes out onto dry land, walks among the coconut trees <note>original: *shajar al-narjil*</note>, and drinks from them. When the whale reaches a certain age, they call it the **Four-Part Shark** <note>original: *al-Jarjur al-Rub’*</note> because its belly becomes rounded with young. For this reason, <unclear>they are born from its sides... the most wondrous thing found was a piece of amber without a flaw... and what was found of it in the box</unclear> <margin><unclear>The Monofite</unclear></margin>

...is blackened, and its poison does not affect the fish entirely. Rather, it is permissible to use the oil from its innards for heat; they also strike the rock until it is level with its brain. There is no cure for the bite of the **Sea-Horse** or the **Sea-Dog**, but they take <note>possibly for medicinal or tool-making use</note> from the bones of their spines.

In this sea, there is a fish called the **Sakan** <note>original: *Al-Sakan*</note>. It is mentioned as living among small stones; its heat carries various poisons. Its gallbladder is of no use unless mixed with the juice of a plant found in the land of the **Zanj** <note>The Zanj refers to the inhabitants of the East African coast</note>. This fish has a body like the marrow of a bird, four legs, and a frame like that of a lion. Its scales are as wide as a dirham <note>a silver coin</note>, and its edges, head, and scales are all jet black. It is harder than ivory and more beautiful than silver. From it, they carve stones for rings and inlay decorative belts.

In the Sea of China, there is an animal that, when brought out onto land, its color becomes enchanted. It is called the **Indian Crab** <note>original: *Saratan al-Hindi*</note> and is used in the preparation of kohl <note>traditional eye medicine or eyeliner</note>.

In the Nile of Egypt and the Sea of Syria, there is a fish called the **Electric Ray** <note>original: *Al-Ra'ada*, meaning 'The Thunderer' or 'The Trembler'</note>. It is small and flat like a board. If a person touches it while they are ill, they will tremble until it is known, and they may fall into a fever. One cannot even hold it while it is alive, and it causes a great deal of disruption.

In the sea of Alexandria, there is a fish called the **Sarab**. It is white in color except for its crest, which is red-tailed, and its head is shaped like a beak. If a person eats it, they see in their dreams as if they are coupling with a multitude, being visited by many, and they see terrifying and frightening visions.

In the Sea of China, there is a fish that the tides cast upon the shore. It walks on its belly, struggling in the mud for half a day, then it slips into the rocks. It has fins on its back and underside that it uses until it returns to the sea.

There is a fish called the **Lantern** <note>original: *Al-Qandil*; likely a jellyfish</note>. Its body is round without a head, and its color is like glass. It has legs like ropes.

There is a fish called the **Blacksmiths** <note>original: *Haddadun*; likely the sawfish</note>. It is round, but its upper snout is as long as a sword—six cubits, or five, or more or less. It has hard, sharp teeth with which it strikes other fish, cutting them into small pieces before swallowing them. Sometimes it strikes small boats and breaks them.

There is a fish called the **Crow** <note>original: *Al-Ghurab*</note>. It has a beak like a crow's beak and two wings with which it flies <note>the flying fish</note>.

There is a fish called the **Hedgehog** <note>original: *Al-Qunfudh*</note>, which is shaped exactly like a land hedgehog.

There is a fish called the **Dolphin** <note>original: *Al-Dulfin*</note>. It resembles a shield. It carries its young inside its precious belly and brings its mouth to the shore.

There is a fish called the **Al-Bus**. It has a face like a human face, and its skin is colored like a peacock, gathering all colors. If it is boiled in water, it tastes sour, but if it is roasted over fire, it is sweet.

There is a fish called the **Shark** <note>original: *Al-Qirsh*</note>. It has a mane like a horse's mane made of bone. It is one cubit long and black in width. Its thickness is like twisted threads. It blows its hair intentionally like a reef; when it commands the other fish, it shakes them and terrifies them.

There is a fish called the **Boxfish** <note>original: *Al-Sanduq*</note>. Its body is square and flat <gloss>armed</gloss> like a turtle. On its head is a horn like a deer's horn. When the **Wala** <note>likely the Whale; original: *Al-Wala*</note> becomes tyrannical due to its great size in the sea, its ailment is cured by this **Boxfish**. It enters the whale's nose and strikes its brain with its horn. The whale does not stop thrashing until it perishes.

In the Sea of Makran, there is a fish called the **Lejam** that swallows people whole.

In the land of the Romans, at the edge of the lands of **Al-Andalus** <note>Muslim Iberia</note>, a great fish emerges every year. It comes easily, and the people take from its meat whatever they desire and love. Then it returns to the sea. It is said that in that land, people benefit from the finest parts of that red meat. It has a very strong scent.

In the Indian Sea, there is a fish that, when its belly is cut open, another fish is found inside. If that one is cut open, another is found inside, and so on without end.

In the sea, there is a creature called the **Mermaids** <note>original: *Banat al-Ma'*, literally 'Daughters of the Water'</note>. They resemble women and have long black hair. Their skin color—glory be to Him—inclines toward yellow. they possess female anatomy and are of great size. They speak in a way that can hardly be understood and laugh from their throats with a viscous sound. Sometimes they dive into the depths of the sea. Some sailors may cohabit with them, which is a great wonder. They do not often emerge from the sea, and at certain times, they may become confused or frantic.

...Abu Musa <note>likely referring to a traditional narrator or scholar</note>, because they set foot in the land of Alexandria and at al-Ramni and the sound of the sons of Tyre. ### Section Twenty-One: Concerning Deformed Human Creation

The Commander of the Faithful, Ali—peace be upon him—described in his famous sermon on the various species the nations that God Almighty created. The Commander of the Faithful named them after the subservient nations, describing their forms and how they differ. Among these deformed creatures from those nations are the inhabitants of **Barhaman** in the Indian Sea. They have **black faces** and are shaped like the people of ‘Ad <note>a legendary giant race mentioned in the Qur'an</note>; however, their feet are reversed, with their heels in front and their toes behind. They are a cubit in height, with graying hair and beardless, elongated faces. They are said to devour any men who fall among them, though they follow the women. 

In the land of the **Zanj** <note>the East African coast</note>, there are creatures created in the image of humans but with tails like small dogs. They have eyes like those who dwell by the sea. In the school of Jasun <note>possibly a corruption of a Chinese city name</note>, one of the cities of China, there are creatures who speak the speech of monkeys. In the islands of India, there is a nation whose faces are in their chests; they have large ears, and each individual possesses both female and male reproductive organs. Their speech cannot be understood. 

Near China, in a place called Sayh al-Har, and appearing from the lands of the Hormuzans, are black people whose height is only four spans. They board ships and drum upon them, yet they do no harm to anyone. Then they return to the sea. When they appear, the sailors know that the sea is about to surge with storms, so they prepare for it. 

In the land of **Wabar** <note>a legendary lost region in the Arabian desert</note>, there are creatures who look as if God’s wrath has fallen upon them. Their forms were transformed until they became as they are now. Among them, a person or a woman has only half a head, half a face, one eye, one hand, and one leg. They hop upon their faces and graze in those forests at the edges of the sea. Their lands have been overrun by predatory ants—the Sabatila ants—which can snatch a rider from his horse. It is said that the jinn have overcome their dwellings, and the King of the Ants has taken their livestock.

->As the poet al-Farazdaq said:<-

->You have gone astray, O Abu Bakr, seeking the house of Darim,<-
->Just as one goes astray seeking the path to Wabar.<-
->He shall never be guided, no matter how hard he tries,<-
->To a known path or even to any tracks.<-

If one approaches the land of Wabar, a great fortress appears, along with vineyards and springs. But as one draws near, it vanishes or turns to dust in one's face. If one attempts to enter, they may be choked or killed. It is said that these humans are of the offspring of **Nasnas** <note>a legendary race of "half-humans"</note> son of Umaym son of Tahyel son of Barlawi son of Shem. 

<margin>The Nasnas</margin> They live between Wabar, the Land of Trees, and the borders of Yemen. They destroy crops, and the people of those lands hunt them with dogs. One trustworthy witness said: "We lost our way and came upon a thicket on the seashore that had no visible end. There we saw a tall creature, like a palm tree, but with only half a body, running as fast as a horse, and he was reciting:

->I fled from the fear of the nobles, running...<-
->When I found no alternative but to flee.<-
->I remember a time in my youth when I was strong,<-
->While today I am very weak.<-

Another said: "I encountered the Nasnas and saw their strange nature." He remembered that when they are hunted, they are called by their names. They brought a creature with half a face and one hand in his chest and one leg. He said to me, "I seek refuge in God and in you!" So I said, "Let him go." They released him and prepared lunch. His companion asked, "Did you return from your hunt with anything?" They replied, "Yes, we caught one, but your boy released it." They began to complain about the lost catch while the creature ran with them. Then a voice from one of the trees called out:

->The time for hunting has passed, the morning has dawned,<-
->The hedgehog has retired to its rest, so put away your bow-string.<-

**The Bawafir**: A creation that appears as a mix between humans and wild land animals. they have long heads, long eyes, and tanned skin. **And a language like the whistling of birds**: This nation consists of people born from the union of fire and sea creatures. They live in the farthest West. They have small eyes and naked bodies. **The Ahwash Rama**: These are born from Gog and Magog <note>Ya'juj and Ma'juj, the legendary tribes of the apocalypse</note>. They are groups with wide faces and ears, looking like desert-dwellers. These belong to a nation born of Gog and Magog who have tails and four hands. Each hand has claws. Among these nations are those who use weapons 

**Resembling swords** with a single edge. In their language, they are called "The Followers" because a hand emerges from the back, and in the other two hands are knives. They fight a nation called the Lu'as. These are a short people with small heads and large ears. They spread their ears out like blankets and eat one another. They hunt a wild beast they have called the Mats, which is 

**In the form of a tent**. They eat its meat at a place near a mountain on the sea called Nah. They have five hands with spread fingers and hooked claws, and a single leg with ten toes. They are quick and hunt sea creatures, which they eat, and their children are exactly like them. They are a nation with the appearance of the Turks. They live in the East of the world at the rising of the sun, near the place known as Kankandar. They are descended from the sons of the bear and wild beasts. They have round, glistening eyes and sharp, protruding tusks. When they retract their claws, they are hooked and sharp. They have short fingers. No other nation lives beyond them to the East, nor to the West of them. Their dwellings are in the mountains and temples. Their food consists of sea creatures and they eat dragons. They have crops and animals that they ride, and they possess wealth.

**The nation is a nation of the Sudan**: They are called the Damdam. Their dwelling is in the region from the South to the West. They eat one another and have sharpened tusks. They possess such might, power, and strength that they surpass all other nations. Gold is plentiful among them; it appears in their land by bursting forth from the sand. They have a great desire for a type of vulture <note>original: "al-jawari"</note> that they train. These birds are set upon meat in their land... and they use them to retrieve gold from its mines.

**The Lidama are Sudan**: They are tall and possess great courage, might, and strength. Their eyes glisten, and they are so tall that hair hardly grows on their width. They eat one another. They have no king, nor are they ruled, except by their own right hands <note>meaning they live by force or individual strength</note>. In their country, the sources of gold are many; they find it scattered like seeds. They are among the furthest of people from the normal 

**Human form. And we found in the letter of Alexander to Aristotle**: <note>Referring to the "Secretum Secretorum" or the "Alexander Romance" tradition</note> He says that in the land of India, there are men who do not graze like cattle, but have the faces of women with beards. Their food is fish and they speak a language that is not understood. We saw trees from which a delicious blood flows. Near them were beings whose faces were like the faces of apes <note>original: "al-ghubar," likely referring to baboons or macaques</note>, with ears of a very white color. And in the sea of Oufash <note>possibly a corruption of Abyssinia or a specific mythical sea</note> are creatures with bodies like the "nation of fire."

<summary>This page catalogs various monstrous and legendary humanoid races, including the cannibalistic Damdam of Africa and the offspring of Gog and Magog, concluding with wonders of India cited from the pseudo-epigraphical correspondence of Alexander the Great.</summary>

...the river and the ships. We saw near India a people whose faces are like the faces of lions, and in their hands, they carry something like shackles. We also saw in the land of India a people with no heads, whose eyes are in their chests and whose mouths are in their livers <note>original: "kabidihim", likely meaning the abdominal area</note>. They speak with the speech of humans, but their bodies are the bodies of small children. Their food is the truffle <note>original: "al-kama'ah", desert truffles common in the Middle East and North Africa</note>, which they treat as if they were melons. ۝

## Chapter Twenty-Two: On the Wonders of Waters

In Azerbaijan, there is a spring of water that is hollowed out like a basin. It is divided into two halves: in one half, a walnut dropped in will be cooked <note>The text implies a boiling heat or a chemical reaction</note>, while the other half is as cold as ice. Whoever drinks of its coldness [is refreshed]. 

In Gorgan <note>A historical region southeast of the Caspian Sea</note>, there is a spring that produces a great amount of water. It emerges every year for five days, appearing at the decline of the sun <note>noon</note>. A piece of wood continues to float upon the water for forty days. Boys swim there and spend the night in the water. When the days of the water's increase have passed, the wood sinks back into the place from which it appeared, and no one is able to take it. It was once bound with iron chains to the mountains of Maqula <unclear>from the branches of vines</unclear>, but it snapped them and disappeared. When the following year came, it reappeared at the time of the water's rising. 

There is a mosque that enters the sea, appearing to people on certain days. They pray in it and then depart, after which the sea covers it again. In the land of Fars <note>Persia</note>, there is a well; when the wind blows strongly, it acts as a great fountain through an opening in its bottom, until the water is returned to the hollow. No one has seen its like. In the district of Arrajan, there is a well that <unclear>the people</unclear> of Arrajan tried to measure the depth of, but they were unable to do so. It overflows with water daily. 

It is called the Land of <unclear>al-Uriyyah</unclear>. In the highlands near it, in what is known as India, there is a place between two mountains from which smoke emerges. They treat it with caution because no one can truly know its nature; if anything damp falls into it, it is consumed by fire. In the district of Iz-Shahr, there is a spring of water; whoever drinks from it is prevented from sexual intercourse <note>original: "jima'", implying temporary impotence or a loss of desire</note>. He will not feel desire for drink or company, and if a hundred people drink from it, all one hundred will be similarly affected. ۝ 

In the region of Darzanja, if a garment is washed in the water, it turns green, yet the water itself is sweet and drinkable. 

<margin>A delicate basin made of stone; its design resembles legs. It is found in a place</margin> known as <unclear>Bahrain</unclear> al-Qubaysiyyan. Because of its dye, it is known to have a hundred [lambs?] <unclear>drink</unclear> from it and it suffices them. Even if a thousand sheep drink from it, it suffices them. The shepherds water their livestock from it, yet the water never decreases nor increases. ۝

In Tiberias <note>In modern-day Israel/Palestine, famous for its hot springs</note> and other cities, there are springs whose currents are very strong. **Ptolemy** <note>The Greek-Roman geographer and astronomer</note> said: <gloss>In the inhabited world, there are springs which, if a person approaches them, he fears</gloss> consumption <note>original: "al-sallal", likely referring to a wasting disease like tuberculosis</note>. There are also springs which, if a person approaches them, the water there becomes like the <unclear>petrified</unclear> earth found on a shore. And the Dead Sea <note>original: "al-mayyit sirr"</note>, if it intends to swallow someone, it traps the person and kills them. 

In the districts of Alexandria, there is a pool from the salt sea, very large in size. Inside it are pebbles; in the heart of every pebble are grains <note>possibly referring to fossils or geodes</note>. 

They move like the movement of grass. If a pregnant woman touches them, her labor and desire will not be harmed. These stones are driven by the waves, and we extract them from the water. The wilderness attracts the stranger by its wonders. Ismail bin Harb tested this place; he planted a branch in the side of the mountain and left it for a day and a night. When he extracted it, he found the water had woven a ring of stone around it. He tried to break it for Qasim, but it would not break. 

Along the road to the West <note>al-Maghrib</note>, there are waters of various colors. 

<margin>In the luminous places are springs whose water is sour and whose colors are green, and waters whose taste is tart</margin> 

There are colors like <term>saffron</term> <note>original: "usfur", bastard saffron/safflower used for yellow/orange dye</note> and colors like <term>cinnabar</term> <note>original: "zinjifur", a bright red mineral pigment</note>. There are bitter waters, and waters with the smell of <unclear>sulfur</unclear>. Some waters, if stored and heated, [change their nature]. There is a water which, if drunk, causes worms to exit the body. There is a medicinal water which, if a person drinks from it on the first day of October <note>original: "Tishrin al-Awwal"</note>, it purges him for the entire year. Its wonderful name is "The Year." 

Among the wonders of the "Reverse Sea" is that it [contains] fire that burns the water, yet they do not mix. You see its color shining, but the bodies are not submerged in it. In a village of Azerbaijan called al-Numan, a great and wondrous fire appears in the middle of its water, preventing the coldness of the water from extinguishing it. Here are the wonders of the Earth. ۝

# Chapter Twenty-Three: On Rare and Strange Plants

In the land of the Zanj <note>original: "Bilad al-Zanj," referring to the Swahili coast of East Africa</note>, there is a tree that grows on a single trunk, its head rising with four branches emerging from a single point. In the center of these branches is a single, upright stem. It bears fruit similar to a citron <note>a large, thick-skinned citrus fruit</note>, stuffed with something like carded silk, resembling the usher tree <note>Calotropis procera, known for its silky seed fibers</note>. What is inside is harvested—it contains seeds like fine flour—and it is used to stuff mattresses and pillows. Its beauty is highly praised; whenever a person sits on it, the stuffing rises and softens until the mattress or pillow becomes full. No one can stuff them to their full capacity except by a specific measure; when someone sleeps on it, the stuffing yields and then settles until it reaches its full volume again. If the mattress or pillow is opened, the stuffing within can be washed; it does not mat together nor does it spoil. I once sat and conversed upon such a thing at the home of a venerable man among our brothers known as Abu al-Qasim al-Harrani, who traveled extensively to the land of the Zanj. I examined it myself and saw a wonder.

The servant Al-Marzuban <note>A Persian title for a provincial governor or noble</note>, nicknamed Ba‘lakan, told me that in the land of Nubia <note>the region of the middle Nile valley</note>, he witnessed a tree that the Nubians revere. No one else has seen a tree like it; its height is one hundred cubits <note>approx. 150 feet</note>, and its shade is wide enough for a hundred gatherings. It is perfectly straight with a smooth trunk. At its top, it has a few leaves resembling palm fronds. It bears fruit like the large melons of Burullus <note>a region in Egypt famous for its melons</note>. The fruit shrinks within its casing as it matures. The noble shepherds and those who recognize this fruit do not seek shade under the tree for fear that some of the fruit might fall on them; for if it falls upon a living creature, it kills them. The fruit remains in this state until it ripens and falls on its own. It is then carried to the King of Nubia, who presents it as a gift to other kings for its deliciousness. I have eaten of this fruit, and I have never seen anything on the face of the earth sweeter or more pleasant. Its taste is like <unclear>al-matkh</unclear> <note>possibly a specific preserve or local fruit name</note>.

In the land of India, there is a palm that bears choice fruit, but it is unique. It is tapped, and what flows from it is collected. If drunk immediately, it is sweet and burns the lips with its sweetness. If left for a short while, it becomes like honey, intoxicating and pleasing to the soul. If it stays for most of the day, it turns into vinegar.

In the region of Shahrar, there is a fragrant herb called the "Rajraj Lily" and <unclear>Qadamshal</unclear>. Inside the lily, there are spots like the eyes of a <unclear>barkhas</unclear> <note>likely an insect or small animal</note>. 

In Al-Mansura, in the land of Sindh <note>Present-day Pakistan</note>, there are dates the size of chicken eggs called "al-Maymuna." They are extremely sour and are used to treat yellow bile. There is also a fruit resembling a peach, which is considered its equal, with a taste close to the peaches found in the district of Satlabih in the Baqahah; half of it is sour and the other half is as sweet as honey.

### The Waq-Waq Tree

In the Waq-Waq Island <note>A legendary island in medieval Arabic geography, variously identified with Madagascar, Sumatra, or Japan</note>—which is in the direction of the Zanj islands—there are trees that bear fruit in the shape of women. They are reddish, hanging by their hair. They are like reddish <unclear>embalming spices</unclear>. They weep and <unclear>cry out</unclear>, and their hands <unclear>pluck at things</unclear> while they scream, "Waq Waq!" If they are cut or fall, they die and no longer speak. In those places, their <unclear>hips</unclear>, backs, faces, and limbs are covered in a fine down like feathers. Whenever a person comes to the island, they find these bodies to be reddish and smooth, with beautiful faces and private parts that never fade. However, if one <unclear>cohabits</unclear> with them, they die within a day or part of a day, even before their speech and beauty cease.

Henna is a tree found in the land of the Sudan, specifically in the region of Kuku. There is a tree there <note>original: "the first king's tree"</note> that reacts to whoever sits beneath it. If a person sits in its midst and takes an oath <unclear>regarding a companion</unclear>, its branches entangle him. If he has sworn truthfully, the branches do not wind around him. 

In Upper Egypt <note>Al-Sa'id</note>, there is a tree called the "Mouse Tree," with prominent "eyes" and soft texture. Its nature is such that if a little is placed in <unclear>a hole</unclear>, it becomes <unclear>poisonous</unclear>. 

In Yemen, there are trees whose leaves and branches <unclear>are called Bananya</unclear>. Their seeds and sap are <unclear>spoiled</unclear>. Yet, if someone says to the tree, "Peace be upon you," it responds by changing the color of its blossoms and flowers to a golden hue.

<summary>This page catalogs various botanical wonders from the medieval world, ranging from practical descriptions of Kapok-like silk trees in East Africa to the legendary Waq-Waq tree whose fruit resembles human women.</summary>

# Chapter Twenty-Four: On the Wonders of Beasts

In the **Land of the Sudan** <note>original: "Bilad al-Sudan," historically referring to the regions of sub-Saharan Africa</note>, there is a beast called the **Al-Mar’i** <note>literally "The Grazer"</note>. Its male cannot be distinguished from its female. It is among the most beautiful of wild animals. It happens that it sometimes sees its own reflection in the moonlight and flees from it; whenever its reflection appears, it runs until it perishes from exhaustion. Also in the Land of the Sudan are the **human-whales** <note>original: "hitan al-insan," likely a reference to large man-eating aquatic creatures or sharks</note> which strike humans with their tails and destroy them. The **Al-Ghalm** is a beast of enormous body, though smaller than an elephant; it has a long neck mottled with red, yellow, green, and white. Kings hunt them and ride them. The **Farsab** is a predatory beast like a lion found in the land of **China**; it is even stronger and more ferocious than a lion. **Al-Lawz meat** <note>original: "Lahm al-Lawz," likely a corruption of a foreign animal name</note> has multi-colored fur like aloes <note>original: "al-sabr," likely referring to the variegated patterns of the aloe plant</note>; from its fur, garments are fashioned that are so fine they can be folded until they disappear. The **wild dog** <note>original: "kalb barri"</note> is found in the **Land of the Romans** <note>original: "Bilad al-Rum," referring to the Byzantine Empire</note>; its meat is eaten. It is also found in the **Maghreb** <note>North Africa</note>, where it is known as the "Back-Dog" <note>original: "kalb al-dhahar"</note>. The **wild ewe** <note>original: "al-jam’a al-barriya"</note> is found in the Land of the Romans; its meat is eaten and fine garments are made from its wool. It is white in color. The **Bunayta** is like a mouse and is found in the land of China; it hunts vipers and vermin like the **Jalalah** <note>likely a type of beetle or scavenger</note>. It has fur like the fur of the... <unclear>faded</unclear>... of the Maghreb. In China, there is also a beast like a fox that hunts snakes... <unclear>faded</unclear>.

The **Al-Mu’ashar** is a beast like a rabbit, but superior in color; it is found in the lands of the West. The **Giraffe** <note>original: "al-zirafah"</note> is of many colors, with an extremely long neck and short hindquarters; it has horns and its ears are like those of a cow. The **Sannajah**—which is the **Nubian Elephant** <note>This description clearly refers to the **Hippopotamus**</note>—is found at the edge of the Sudan; it has four hooves like the feet of a duck, a mane like that of a horse, skin like a buffalo's, a long tail, and a wide mouth that looks as if it were a sieve. It grazes on crops and dives into the water whenever it wishes, even walking on the seabed. The kings of **Nubia** display them just as other kings keep horses in their stables. The **Hadman** is a large predatory beast like a dog, extremely strong, and devours everything; it is found in the **Land of the Turks** <note>Central Asia</note>. The **Banush** are wild dogs found in the Land of the Romans. The **Fayramarah** is a beast like a ewe found in the wilderness of the Land of the Romans; it is hunted and its meat is eaten. It has fur from which fine **Baranawi** <note>a type of high-quality woolen textile</note> is made; it comes in four colors: black, white, grey, and yellow.

The **Kattan** is a wicked beast like a buffalo, black in color with a mane like a ram; it is found in the coastal wildernesses. The **Bahlamas** is a beast with two wings like those of an ostrich and a horn on its nose; it has the body of a bull but is much larger. The elephant flees from it, for if the Bahlamas strikes it with its horn, it kills it. No other predators stay in the places where it dwells, nor do any trees grow there, for its breath is poisonous and deadly to animals and burns clothing. The **Juju**—which is the **Rhinoceros** <note>original: "al-karkadann"</note>—is found in the lands of **India** and Nubia. Its voice is like that of a black horse with a white blaze on its forehead and white markings on its legs. It has small ears, and on its forehead is a single horn, round at the base and pointed at the tip. This horn is soft when the beast is calm and hardens when it is angry. When the horn is split open, one may see images within it: the figure of a horse, a man, a beast, or a bird. These images appear in black on a white ground, or sometimes the horn shows a green image on a red ground. The people of China use the "hearts" <note>the inner core</note> of these horns to make handles for <unclear>knives</unclear> and belts.

The **Hartaqah** is similar to the elephant but smaller in build and stature. The elephant flees from it. Like the elephant <note>according to common medieval belief</note>, it has no joints in its forelegs or hindlegs. The **Badah** is a beast resembling a donkey with a long neck and black stripes; it is found in the lands of the <unclear>South</unclear>. The **Salhouf** is a beast larger than a dog, shaped like a rabbit with a head like an ostrich; it is found in the wilderness of the Land of the <unclear>Turks</unclear>.

# Chapter Twenty-Four: On the Wonders of Wild Beasts

In the land of the Sudan <note>Historically referring to the "Land of the Blacks," spanning sub-Saharan Africa from the Atlantic to the Nile.</note>, there is a beast called the **al-Mar’i**. It is not known which are male or female; it is of the most beautiful wild beasts, having a <unclear>skin</unclear> that is smooth. Often, when it sees a person in the wilderness, it flees, and whenever a person appears to it, it runs until it perishes. Also in the land of the Sudan are numerous snakes that destroy a person while they are asleep. The **al-Ghalam** <note>likely the Giraffe, from the description of the neck and markings.</note> is a beast of immense body, though smaller than an elephant. It has a long neck adorned with red, yellow, and white markings. Kings hunt them and ride them. The **al-Farsa** <note>original: "al-Farsa bi-"</note> is a predator like a lion found in the land of China; it is stronger and more arrogant than a lion due to its great strength. The kings of China obtain garments made from its fur. There is a wild creature caught in the wilderness that is a smaller version of the cat; it is a wild dog found in the land of Rome <note>referring to the Byzantine Empire or Mediterranean Europe.</note>. Its meat is eaten and it is known for its good qualities, though it is the most dog-like of dogs. The **al-Tayma** is a Roman wild sheep <note>original: "al-rujmah al-barriyya"</note> found in the lands of Rome; it is eaten, and from its wool, high-quality garments are fashioned. In the lands of the **Nun** <note>possibly a regional name or a transcription error for a specific Chinese province.</note>, there is a small beast resembling a mouse found in the land of China; it hunts people and vermin alike. There is also a fur-bearing animal <unclear>...</unclear> like the **al-Mu’arrab** <note>meaning "The Arabized" or perhaps a corruption of a local name.</note> cat found in the land of China, which resembles a fox and is used to hunt other foxes. The **al-Mu’wash** <note>original: "al-Mawur al-Ma'ush"</note> is a beast like a rabbit that <unclear>is lost in the light</unclear>; it is found in the "Region of the Moon" <note>Aqlīm al-Qamar, a legendary geographical zone in medieval Islamic maps, often associated with the sources of the Nile.</note>. The **al-Nazah** is a creature with <unclear>distinctive features</unclear> in a long land; it has a very long neck, short legs, horns, and tails like the tails of cows.

The **al-Sannajah** <note>The Hippopotamus.</note>, which is the "Water-Horse of Nubia," is found on the edges of Sudan. They have four webbed feet like the feet of a duck, manes like the manes of horses, skins like the skins of buffaloes, elongated tails, and wide mouths. It is said that it destroys crops and can swim in the water if it wishes, or walk on the bottom of the sea if it wishes. It is brought out to the land for the kings of Nubia, and some kings keep them in their stables. The wild donkey, the **al-Fartan**, follows humans and is like a dog; its strength begins with a period of weakness. It is found in the lands of the Turks. The **Yadnush** are wild dogs found in the lands of Rome. The **al-Fabrah** is a beast like a ewe found in the wildernesses; it is hunted and its meat is eaten. It has wool from which high-quality "Baranuy" <note>A type of fine fabric.</note> is made, coming in four colors: black, white, grey, and yellow.

The **al-Karkadann** <note>The Rhinoceros.</note> is a beast like a buffalo, black in color, with <unclear>...</unclear> like that of a ram; it is found in the wildernesses of the Zanj <note>East Africa.</note>. The **al-Bantus** <note>Likely the mythical Bonnacon.</note> is a beast with two wings like the wings of an ostrich, and it has the body of a bull, though it is much larger and more massive. It flees from the elephant. When it exhales from its <unclear>nose</unclear>, it is lethal; no predators can live in the place where it dwells, and no trees grow there because its breath is deadly to animals and burns through clothing.

The **al-Hawju** is the Rhinoceros <note>original: "al-Karkadann"</note> found in the lands of India and the lands of Nubia. It has the form of a black horse with a white blaze on its forehead, small ears, and a snout. On the crown of its forehead, it has a single horn, rounded at the base and sharpened at the tip <unclear>...</unclear>. When it is still, it weakens, but it strikes with its horn, gashing wounds into the body. One can hear the sound of <unclear>...</unclear> the wild horse and birds in the black lands. The people of China make belts from its horns, which they value highly and compete for; a single **belt** <unclear>can be worth</unclear> a thousand dinars. Elephants flee from it due to its nature and intelligence; all animals that touch it <unclear>...</unclear>.

The **al-Yarbad** is a large bovine-like beast with a long neck, black in color, and similarly striped; it is found in the lands of <unclear>...</unclear>. A beast called the **Salis** is larger than a dog and shaped like a rabbit; it has a head like <unclear>...</unclear> and prominent ears. It is found in the land of <unclear>...</unclear>.

# The Twenty-Fourth Objective: On Strange Beasts

In the land of the Sudan <note>Historically referring to the "Land of the Blacks," the sub-Saharan belt across Africa</note> is a beast called the *Marm‘a*; it is not known whether it is male or female, and it dwells in the desolate regions. It is so skittish that if a person sees it in the wilderness, it flees, and whenever an enemy appears to it, it runs until it perishes. And in the land of the Sudan is an animal that strikes a man with its tail and kills him. The *Qalam* <note>Likely a variant of *qaylan* or a similar regional name for a giraffe-like animal</note> is a beast of immense body, though smaller than an elephant, with a long neck variegated in red, yellow, green, and white. Kings hunt it and ride it. The *Farsab* is a predator like the lion found in the land of China, but it is stronger than a lion and lon<unclear>ger</unclear>. In the land of Habr <note>Possibly a transcription error for a specific region</note> is a type of predatory beast... weaker than the *Rasnas*. The wild dog is found in the l<unclear>and</unclear> of the Rum <note>Byzantium/Eastern Rome</note>; its meat is eaten and it is also found in the Maghreb, known as the Saharan dog. In the desert sands of the land of the Rum, there is a white animal whose wool is used to make fine garments; no dye can match its whiteness. The *Qarah* is found in the land of China; it is hunted for prof<unclear>it</unclear>. The *Harlam* is similar, with fur the color of mourning. In the Maghreb and China, there is an animal like a fox with which other foxes are hunted. Great is God <unclear>in</unclear> His creation.

The *Mu‘ashir* is a beast like a rabbit, white in color, found in the region of the Qann. The Giraffe is many-colored like feathers, with a very long neck and short <unclear>legs</unclear>. It has horns, and when it kneels, it is like a cow. The *Sannaja* cow <note>Likely a description of the hippopotamus</note>, also called the *Hawl al-Nushah*, is found on the borders of Sind. It has four horns, feet like those of a duck, a mane like a horse's mane, and skin like buffalo hide. It has long tails and wide mouths, as if it were <unclear>made</unclear> for the fields. It swims in the water when it wishes and walks on the bottom of the sea when it wishes. It is brought to the kings of Nubia and <unclear>kept</unclear> as kings keep wild asses in their stables. The *Aframan* follows the ram and is extremely strong, resembling a leopard in every way; it is found in the la<unclear>nd</unclear> of the Turks. The *Balanush* are wild dogs found in the land of the Rum. The *Fabrash* is a beast like a ewe found in the wilderness of <unclear>Abyssinia</unclear>. It is hunted and its meat is eaten, and it has wool of the highest quality... of four colors: black, white, red, and yellow.

The *Kubbah al-Sharh* is a beast like a buffalo, black in color, with a tool like a ram’s; it is found in the coastal wilderness. The *Bahnas* is a beast <unclear>that has</unclear> two wings like those of an ostrich, with a head and nose like a bull’s, though its body is larger and grander. Other be<unclear>asts</unclear> flee from it; when it passes by, it is recognized from afar, and no predators stay in the place where it dwells, nor do any forbidden plants grow there, for its breath is dea<unclear>th</unclear> and destructive to animals. The "Mountain Wave," which is the <term>Karkadann</term> <note>The Rhinoceros; the name is Persian in origin</note>, is found in the land of India and the land of Nubia. It <unclear>is</unclear> like a black horse with a white blaze on its forehead, small ears, and a snout. On the top of its forehead is a single horn, round at the base and pointed <unclear>at the tip</unclear>. It is weak when the beast is calm and hardens when it is angry. If one of its horns is split, one find within the circles of the horn images <unclear>such as</unclear> the likeness of a human, a peacock, black earth, green lands, and trees in black earth, and green souls, and images of trees <unclear>in red earth</unclear>, and green images in red earth. The people of China make belts from the sections of its horns, in which they find delight, and a belt is sold for five thousand dinars. It is smaller than the elephant in stature, but the elephant flees from it. Like the elephant, it has no joints in its le<unclear>gs</unclear>. The <unclear>wild</unclear> beast in the west is like a donkey with a long neck, black and white, and also striped... except in the interior of the country <unclear>and it</unclear> is called the *Basbus*. It is a beast larger than a dog, shaped like a rabbit, but its head is like a human head; it is found in the land of the Turks.

<summary>This page catalogs various exotic and mythical animals from the Sudan, China, Byzantium, and India, including a detailed description of the Rhinoceros (Karkadann) and its prized horn. It reflects medieval "wonders" literature where real animals like the giraffe and hippo are blended with legendary beasts.</summary>

<keywords>zoology, bestiary, Karkadann, rhinoceros, giraffe, Sudan, China, medieval science, wonders of creation</keywords>

<meta>This page describes the exterior of the manuscript. As the original surface contains no legible text or inscriptions, this translation focuses on the physical characteristics and artistry of the binding itself to provide context for the volume.</meta>

# The Binding

-> **Exterior Cover** <-

This manuscript is encased in a **weathered, dark brown leather binding**, a style characteristic of historical Islamic bookmaking. The surface of the leather is heavily aged, bearing a network of fine cracks, scuffing, and deep discoloration that speaks to its long history and frequent use.

At the heart of the front cover is a central, embossed <term>mandorla<note>An almond-shaped decorative motif, often called a "shamsa" (sunburst) in Islamic art, typically used as a central ornament on book covers.</note></term>. Though the leather is worn, one can still see the remnants of intricate ornamental patterns pressed into this medallion. Extending from the top and bottom of this central circle are smaller, vertically aligned decorative stamps known as <term>pendants<note>Subordinate decorative elements that "hang" from the main central motif to elongate the design.</note></term>.

The spine is also bound in dark leather, and at the corners, the material has worn away through centuries of handling, exposing the underlying board material.

<summary>This page documents the manuscript's aged leather binding, featuring a traditional central medallion (mandorla) and decorative pendants typical of Islamic book arts.</summary>
<keywords>binding, leather, mandorla, shamsa, pendants, book arts, Islamic manuscript</keywords>
